Round-3 review of the second codex revision of the design for issue #4525 (positron-ai/tron)

Did the second revision close the round-2 findings, and is the typed KV-cache tensor design implementable now?

Reviewed 2026-09-22 UTC by Claude (Fable 5.1) for jhan. Design reviewed: status/design-new-tensor-type.html, second revision by codex after the round-2 review (finalized 2026-09-22 06:24 UTC, 121,774 bytes, sha256 5dea1702). Sources pinned to main f46e48ba (the design's snapshot), main 2880c3aa9b (the tip the design's S15-S20 cite), the live main tip 1400fa4481 (fetched 2026-09-22 ~07:00 UTC; none of the design's files changed since 2880c3aa9b) and PR 4424 head 30c4ac82cb.
Short version

The second revision resolved 60 of the 87 open items (64 round-2 findings plus 23 round-1 residuals). 25 are partly resolved, 2 are deferred to jhan, none is unresolved and none regressed.

This round found 52 new defects: 1 major, 27 minor, 24 notes. The major one is a single missing sentence: the design no longer says how detail::scaled_v_expr, the weighted V sum in kv_cache.hpp, obtains the packed address under the new access rule (G01). The other items an implementer would stop on are the t_amx_numerics fixture route (G22), the constraint mechanism (G09) and the D5 wording (G04).

Verdict: accept with changes. All three judges say so; two of three say the design is not yet implementable until G01 and G22 are written. After those two edits and the eleven regressions and residuals listed in section 7, another agent can implement the parent from this page.

1. Words used here

tron
the inference program under test; its source is the positron-ai/tron repository
PR 4424, the child
the open draft pull request "VNNI K: store the K cache of 128-dimension heads in the AMX VNNI layout, with a shared block save" (branch jhan-amx-vnniK, head 30c4ac82cb). After the new parent PR exists, PR 4424 is rebased onto it and is then called the child
the parent PR
the new pull request that issue #4525 asks for: typed access to the existing V and K caches on main, with no new layout
Ben, the reviewer
the tron maintainer who reviewed PR 4424 on 2026-09-21 (a worker-index question, a review asking for typed tensors, and an API sketch). The revised design calls him "the reviewer"
issue #4525
the GitHub issue codex created for the parent PR; jhan edited its title and first sentence on 2026-09-22 00:10 UTC; unchanged since
KV cache, K, V
the saved key (K) and value (V) vectors of earlier tokens; attention reads them
page, slot, head, D
a page holds 64 token positions; a slot is the cache region of one layer (or of several layers that share it); a head is one attention head; D is the head size, the number of values per token per head (128 for PR 4424's models)
bf16
the 16-bit brain floating-point format used for cached K and V
AMX, AVX-512, 16-lane and 8-lane builds
AMX and AVX-512 are CPU instruction sets. tron's vector width TRON_CHUNK_SIZE is 16 fp32 values with AVX-512 (the 16-lane build) and 8 without (the 8-lane build); the 8-lane build stores V row-major, so it has no pair-interleaved V
packed V (pair-interleaved V)
today's V storage in the 16-lane build: for each dimension, tokens 2p and 2p+1 sit next to each other. Element offset = (token/2)*(2*D) + 2*dim + token%2
native K
today's K storage: one token's D values consecutive in memory (row-major)
blocked packed K (VNNI K)
PR 4424's K storage for 128-dimension heads: two consecutive dimensions of one token share a 4-byte unit, and 16 tokens share one 64-byte line
expr, view, const_view, tensor
tron's expression interface (h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp), its typed pointer views over regularly laid-out memory and their read-only form (h/tron/tensor/view.hpp), and its owning arrays (h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp)
kv_block
the struct that holds K then V for one head of one slot in one page (h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp)
set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v, v_data
today's V functions in kv_cache.hpp: write one token, read one token, copy one token between pages, compute the weighted V sum, and return the raw pointer of a V plane
save_v_impl
the model function (h/tron/models/model.hpp) that stores one token's V into the cache during a forward pass; it calls page::set_v
Zero-Initialized V Slots invariant
the rule in kv_cache.hpp that every even-token V write zeroes its odd partner row. The weighted sum reads a whole pair even when the token count is odd, and NaN times 0 is NaN, so the padding row must be zero
arena, book
an arena is one allocated memory region holding cache blocks; book is the class that owns the arenas and the pages
EAGLE
the speculative model; its cache adds one extra physical KV slot and a separate embedding buffer (x_data); it requires uniform geometry
GOF, page_info
group of four: four tokens of K and V staged for the FPGA's hardware KV cache; the staging code reaches a page's rows through the page_info callbacks in h/tron/gof.hpp (namespace tron::hardware)
CI, Nix test build, TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, TRON_K_VNNI
CI is the automated build and test. The Nix test build is the required CI lane. The two CMake options compile the AMX attention kernels and PR 4424's K layout; the Nix lane sets neither. Issue #3997 tracks the AMX gap
codex, the design, the second revision
codex is the agent jhan asked to answer Ben; the design is the page it wrote, status/design-new-tensor-type.html; the second revision is the version finalized 2026-09-22 06:24 UTC (121,774 bytes) after the round-2 review. It is the version reviewed here
round 1, F.. and C.. ids
the first Claude review of the original design (2026-09-22 ~03:00 UTC), archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1.html. Its 58 retained findings are F01-F59 (finders) and C01-C09 (completeness critic)
round 2, N.. and M.. ids
the second Claude review, of the first revision (2026-09-22 ~06:20 UTC), archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r2.html. It graded the 58 round-1 findings (35 resolved, 20 partly, 3 deferred) and added 64 findings N01-N62 (finders) and M01-M08 (critic). The design cites these ids in brackets, for example [N13]
round 3, G.. and H.. ids, the 87 open items
this review. G.. are new findings from the ten finders, H.. from the completeness critic. The 87 open items are the 64 round-2 findings plus the 23 round-1 findings that round 2 graded partly or deferred; section 3 grades each against the second revision
v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view, v_vnni_row, detail::v_vnni_access
the design's names for the packed-V owner (the aligned array inside the cache block), the typed rank-2 view over it, its logical row expression, and the friend helper that alone may read the packed pointer
append_v_row, load_row, copy_token
the design's typed bulk operations: write one token's V row into the packed plane (an even token zeroes its odd partner), read one row out, copy one token between planes. The design adds no copy_plane for V
kv_cache_fwd.hpp, v_vnni.hpp, k_vnni.hpp
the design's new headers: a declarations-only header (no intrinsics) that amx_attn_iface.hpp includes, the V definitions header, and the child's K definitions header under h/tron/tensor/
native_k_view, const_native_k_view
the design's aliases for today's row-major K view and its read-only form (view / const_view with explicit strides Cols, 1); no new K storage class in the parent
k_storage, k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, k_packed
the child's typed K storage as the design specifies it: a std::conditional_t that selects the packed owner for 128-dimension heads and the existing array otherwise, its view, and the accessor that replaces the raw k_vnni_plane pointer
decision record, Q1-Q4, D5
the table in design section 2: four questions for Ben with a proposed choice, a fallback if rejected, and a record status, plus D5, the native-K typing scope that jhan requested
TRON_IGNORE_NAN
a compile definition that tron sets in every non-Debug GCC/Clang build (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:293); with it, the generic bf16 conversion does not preserve NaNs whose upper mantissa bits are zero
page_supports_uniform_kv_access
a compile-time probe in t/t_llama_unit.cpp:105 that checks a page type offers the uniform K/V accessors; the design has it rewritten for the typed get_v destination
8-lane baseline limit
the design's statement that t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler do not compile in the 8-lane build at unmodified main (they call the 16-lane-only set_v/get_v), so the parent's 8-lane acceptance excludes them
RUN_TESTS, passage marker
in the required GCP Nix workflow, RUN_TESTS is true only when the test identity has not passed before or the run is a rerun; the passage marker records a passed identity so a later run may skip the tests
for_each_active_slot, storage_location, slot_base
book members the design reuses to enumerate physical KV slots with their compile-time geometry, to learn which arena a slot lives in, and to compute its address
checker, re-grader, finder, verifier, critic, judge
review agents of this round. A checker graded a batch of known items against the second revision; two re-graders (a source-truth lens and a fairness lens) independently re-graded each; the majority of the three decided. Ten finders looked for new defects; one merge agent removed duplicates; three verifiers tried to refute each candidate; a finding survived when at least two could not. A completeness critic added candidates that went through the same verification. Three judges gave independent implementability verdicts
resolved, partly, deferred, unresolved, regressed
disposition statuses; the table in section 3 gives the meaning of each
blocker, major, minor, note
severities; the table in section 4 gives the meaning of each
detail::scaled_v_expr, the weighted V sum
the struct in kv_cache.hpp that computes the attention-weighted sum of a page's V rows on the AVX-512 path; today it reads the packed plane through a raw pointer. G01 is about how it gets that address under the new types
t_amx_numerics PV case
the unit test case that compares the AMX PV kernel (weights times V) with the AVX path; today it hand-packs a std::vector page and passes its pointer to the kernel
lint-notes
a separate Make target that checks every 'See Note [X]' comment has a matching Note definition; the required Lint CI check runs it, make lint does not

2. What changed between the round-2 and round-3 versions of the design

The second revision keeps the ten-section structure and grows the page by about 46 percent (83,567 to 121,774 bytes; 8,218 to 11,746 words). Every non-ASCII glyph is now a numeric entity. Codex again changed no production code, posted nothing to GitHub, and edited no issue; the header, section 9 and section 10 say so.

The table lists the changes an implementer would notice. The word-level diff (979 changed segments) is saved as evidence/claude-review-r3/design-r2-to-r3-wdiff.txt.

ItemRound-2 version (83,567 bytes)Round-3 version (121,774 bytes, reviewed here)
Short version and header"incorporates the supplied Claude review""incorporates the round-2 design review, including its verifier corrections"; the short version now names the four decisions (own storage type for packed V, layout-specific kernel arguments, native K keeps its storage and view, child adapts to a separate packed-K owner)

Two things did not change. The proposed types, the kernel signatures and the seven-step plan are the same as in round 2. And the design still names the reviewer in its Words table while the text it prepares for the issue uses a role only; that is allowed for a local page (G36).

3. Did the revision address the 87 open items?

Each of the 87 open items (64 round-2 findings and 23 round-1 residuals) was checked against the second revision by one checker agent and then independently re-graded by two adversarial re-graders (a source-truth lens and a fairness lens). The status below is the majority of the three; a split vote (three different statuses) keeps the checker's status and is flagged. Where I (the lead) changed a status after reading the votes, the row says so.

Resolved 60Partly 252
Resolved = the revised text meets the round-1 recommendation in substance, and every checkable fact in the fix is right · Partly = the substance is there, but a concrete part of the recommendation is missing or a small slip remains · Deferred = the design explicitly leaves it to separate work; the remark says whether that is acceptable

4. New findings on this revision

Ten finders read the second revision through one lens each (C++ interface, behavior and arena construction, process and CI, handoff actionability, fidelity to Ben and jhan, simplicity and scope, formulas and page quality, the child's needs, a regression hunt over the round-2 to round-3 word diff, and a section-9 honesty audit). Their 62 candidates and 19 new defects the disposition checkers found in the fixes were merged into 52 candidates, and each was put to three verifiers who tried to refute it; a finding survived when at least two could not. A completeness critic added four candidates that went through the same verification. Two survivors were dropped by the lead (H04 duplicates G18; G52 is a positive check) and nine were regraded after reading the votes; the Lead's reading row of each finding says why. Findings in full

Major G01. scaled_v_expr is not named as a permitted user of detail::v_vnni_access, and its file home was dropped

Where
5. Interface design, Internal storage access row (design-r3.txt:249) and page::scaled_v row (:273); 6. Conversions and weighted sums (:329); 7. step 2 (:373); 8. Definition of done (:496)
Earlier ids
touches M07 N23 N24 N52
Claim
detail::scaled_v_expr is defined inside kv_cache.hpp, which is cache code. Its 16-lane constructor loads whole 64-byte pair tiles with _mm512_load_si512 and feeds them to _mm512_dpbf16_ps. The design gives that constructor a v_vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, head_size>, and the view has no public pointer route (design-r3.txt:248). Line 249 allows detail::v_vnni_access only inside typed tensor operations and V-specific kernels, and says cache callers do not use it. Line 329 says 'Obtain the physical address only inside the typed implementation' without naming a mechanism. The round-3 edit deleted the round-2 sentence that kept scaled_v_expr in kv_cache.hpp. Reading through row.chunk would double the loads and change the instruction stream that line 329 says to preserve. An implementer cannot tell whether (a) scaled_v_expr counts as a V-specific kernel and may call the friend from kv_cache.hpp, or (b) the weighted-sum body must move into h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp as a typed operation that section 5 does not list. The definition-of-done sentence at :496 cannot be checked until that decision is written.
Design says
Use detail::v_vnni_access , a friend declared with the V types, only inside typed tensor operations and V-specific kernels. The child uses a separate detail::k_vnni_access in its own header. Cache callers and tests do not use these helpers. [...] Obtain the physical address only inside the typed implementation.
Evidence
design-r3.txt:249, :273 ('Use a packed read-only V view in the 16-lane constructor'), :329, :373 (step 2 no longer says where scaled_v_expr lives), :496, :39 (Words: 'A kernel performs a specific computation', which does not settle the question). Round-2 text evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:223 ('detail::vnni_access is the internal friend used only by tensor operations and layout-specific kernels') and :353 ('Keep typed scaled_v_expr here unless a real include dependency requires a separate header'); design-wdiff.txt segments @old 2627-2638 (sentence added) and @old 4447 (placement sentence deleted). Source: h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1988-1993 (namespace detail, struct scaled_v_expr, member const bf16s* const data), :2066-2109 (constructor: const bf16x32* tile = reinterpret_cast<const bf16x32*>(data + i * (head_size / chunk_size)); _mm512_dpbf16_ps(t0, _mm512_load_si512(tile + 0), scale); eight tiles per pair for head_size 128), :2000-2007 (even_part/odd_part are private helpers, not the hot-loop read), :2252-2262 (page::scaled_v passes kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).v[0]).
Correction from verification
Narrow the title and kind. Replace "two access rules conflict" with "the helper rule at :249 does not say whether scaled_v_expr counts as a V-specific kernel, and :329 names no mechanism"; the rules conflict only under the reading that scaled_v_expr is a "cache caller". Change kind from contradiction to ambiguity. Fix one citation: namespace detail opens at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1987, so cite :1987-1993 (not :1988-1993). Add the provenance fact: no round-2 finding asked to delete the placement sentence, so its removal (design-wdiff.txt @old 4447) is an unrequested loss of specificity relative to the round-2 text (evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:353). Keep the recommendation. Option A is the one consistent with step 2 (:373 keeps typed weighted-sum inputs in kv_cache.hpp) and with the deleted round-2 sentence, so present it as the default: add to :273 and :373 "detail::scaled_v_expr stays in kv_cache.hpp; its 16-lane constructor takes v_vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, head_size>, obtains the pair-tile base once through detail::v_vnni_access, and keeps today's _mm512_load_si512 / _mm512_dpbf16_ps loops (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2078-2119)", and amend :249 to name scaled_v_expr as the one kv_cache.hpp user of the helper. Drop the sentence "The definition-of-done sentence at :496 cannot be checked until that decision is written"; it can be checked once the implementer records which reading was used, so say instead "the DoD at :496 needs the classification of scaled_v_expr written down to be checkable without a judgment call."
Narrow the title to "scaled_v_expr is not named as a permitted user of detail::v_vnni_access". Drop "two access rules conflict": the rules at design-r3.txt:249 and :329 conflict only if scaled_v_expr is a cache caller, and the design leaves that classification unstated rather than contradicting itself. Drop option (b) from the claim: the design already places the weighted sum in kv_cache.hpp through step 2 ("typed weighted-sum inputs" in the kv_cache.hpp row, design-r3.txt:373), step 1 (v_vnni.hpp contents list no weighted sum, :372) and section 5 (only append_v_row, load_row, copy_token move, :255-259). Corrected claim: "detail::scaled_v_expr stays in kv_cache.hpp (implied by step 2, design-r3.txt:373) and takes a packed read-only V view (design-r3.txt:273). Its 16-lane body loads whole 64-byte pair tiles [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2066-2109], which needs a tile address the view does not expose publicly (design-r3.txt:248). The helper rule at design-r3.txt:249 allows detail::v_vnni_access only in typed tensor operations and V-specific kernels and says cache callers do not use it, and line 329 does not name the helper. The design never says scaled_v_expr counts as a V-specific kernel. The round-3 edit also deleted the explicit placement sentence (design-wdiff.txt @old 4447), so placement is now implied rather than stated." Corrected recommendation, one sentence, added to the page::scaled_v row (design-r3.txt:273) and referenced from the helper row (:249): "detail::scaled_v_expr stays in kv_cache.hpp, counts as a V-specific kernel under the helper rule, and is the only kv_cache.hpp user of detail::v_vnni_access: its 16-lane constructor obtains the pair-tile base once through the helper and keeps today's _mm512_load_si512 and _mm512_dpbf16_ps loop, unrolled tiles and addition order; page, book and tests do not call the helper." Optionally restore the deleted step-2 placement sentence. Severity minor. Relates to N52 and N24 (whose round-2 corrections already read "the typed implementation" at r2:316 as scaled_v_expr's own body) and M07 (helper per layout family, resolved).
Narrow the title to "scaled_v_expr is not named as a permitted user of detail::v_vnni_access, and its file home was dropped". Delete the sub-claims "two access rules conflict" and "the definition-of-done sentence at :496 cannot be checked". Replace "An implementer cannot tell whether (a) or (b)" with "The remaining text points to (a): step 2 places typed weighted-sum inputs under kv_cache.hpp [design-r3.txt:373], section 9 says 'Type both weighted-sum constructors' [:520], and the v_vnni.hpp operation list has no weighted sum [:253-259, :371]; the design should say so in one sentence." Keep recommendation (A) only: add to the page::scaled_v row [design-r3.txt:273] "detail::scaled_v_expr stays in kv_cache.hpp. Its 16-lane constructor obtains the pair-tile base once through detail::v_vnni_access and keeps today's load and dot-product loop. Under the access rule it is a V-specific kernel." and add to line 249 "scaled_v_expr is the one kv_cache.hpp user." Drop option (B), which adds an operation the design deliberately does not list. Drop the pair_tile alternative: a public method that takes pair and tile indices exposes physical indexing through the view, which line 496 confines to tensor operations and kernels. Cite Ben's sketch lines 152 and 209 [github-review.json comments[3]] as the precedent for kernel-side pointer extraction. Severity minor.
Recommendation
Add to the page::scaled_v row (design-r3.txt:273) and repeat in step 2 (:373) ONE of: (A) 'detail::scaled_v_expr stays in kv_cache.hpp. Its 16-lane constructor takes v_vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, head_size>, obtains the pair-tile base once through detail::v_vnni_access, and keeps today's _mm512_load_si512 and _mm512_dpbf16_ps loop, unrolled tiles, and addition order. Under the helper rule it is a V-specific kernel and the only kv_cache.hpp user of the helper; page, book, and tests do not call it.' or (B) 'Move the 16-lane weighted-sum body into h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp as a typed operation weighted_sum(v_vnni_view<const bf16, R, D>, const tensor<R>& scales, size_t count, tensor<D>& scratch); scaled_v_expr's constructor calls it and stores no pointer.' Then change line 249 to name that user explicitly. If neither friend use nor a move is wanted, add to the view contract 'bf16x32 pair_tile(size_t pair, size_t tile) const noexcept' (the 64-byte tile of pair p at dimensions 16*tile .. 16*tile+15, both tokens interleaved; used only by scaled_v_expr and the PV kernel).
Lead's reading
All three verifiers hold the finding and narrow it: the two rules do not contradict each other, because line 249 forbids the helper to "cache callers" and the design never says whether detail::scaled_v_expr is one. The remaining text points to option A: step 2 keeps the typed weighted-sum inputs under kv_cache.hpp [design-r3.txt:373], the v_vnni.hpp operation list has no weighted sum [:253-259, :372], and section 9 says "Type both weighted-sum constructors" [:520]. The round-2 sentence that kept scaled_v_expr in kv_cache.hpp was deleted without any finding asking for it [design-wdiff.txt @old 4447]. The verifiers graded this minor because the reading is recoverable. I keep major: all three judges name it as the one sentence that must exist before implementation starts, and the PV weighted sum runs on every decode step, so two implementers must not resolve it differently. The fix is one sentence at :273 and :373 plus naming scaled_v_expr at :249.
Found by
handoff, cpp-interface; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor G04. D5 says 'reuse the existing view alias'; the alias is new in this design

Where
2. Decision record, row D5 (design-r3.txt:99)
Earlier ids
touches N14 N20 N22 N50
Claim
Row D5 tells the implementer to reuse an existing view alias. No alias named native_k_view or const_native_k_view exists at main or in the child. Section 5 introduces both aliases in the new header h/tron/tensor/kv_cache_fwd.hpp (design-r3.txt:232). What exists is the view type itself, view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>>, which kv_block::k_view() already returns. Section 2's earlier bullet says this correctly ('the existing read-only matrix view', :76). Row D5 contradicts it and could send the implementer looking for an alias in the repository. Row D5 is a new round-3 segment.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
| D5. Who owns native-K typing? | The issue author requested it. Reuse the existing view alias and change the native-K kernel parameter. |
git grep native_k_view at 2880c3aa9b and at 30c4ac82cb over h, src and t: no match. design-r3.txt:232 declares native_k_view and const_native_k_view inside the new kv_cache_fwd.hpp. h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2208-2219 k_view() returns view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>> (non-const) and const_view<...> (const). h/tron/tensor/seq.hpp@2880c3aa9b:388-394 row_major<seq<R,C>> = sseq<C,1>, so the explicit-stride alias names the same type. design-r3.txt:76. design-wdiff.txt: REPLACE @old 1384 / new 1918.
Correction from verification
Narrow one detail: the k_view() citation range is 2207-2219 if the TRON(nodiscard, inline, pure) attribute lines are included, 2208-2219 for the function heads; the row_major machinery spans seq.hpp:377-394 (seq_stride at 377-379, row_major_ specialization at 387-390, alias at 393-394), not only 388-394. The recommendation stands as written; optionally add a section-5 reference in the cell so the implementer finds the alias definition: "The issue author requested it. Reuse the existing native-K view type (the type kv_block::k_view() returns today) through the new const_native_k_view alias declared in section 5, and change the native-K kernel parameter." [design-r3.txt:99, :232, :277; kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2207-2219]

Minor G08. Forward declarations of bf16, view and const_view should be spelled with the struct key

Where
5. Interface design, declarations header paragraph (design-r3.txt:234; block at :232)
Earlier ids
touches F43 N14 N20 N22
Claim
The new sentence tells the implementer to forward-declare bf16, view and const_view as 'classes'. All three are defined with the struct key. A forward declaration with the class key compiles, but clang 19 (the pinned compiler) emits -Wmismatched-tags under -Wall in every translation unit that sees both the forward declaration and the definition. The header is included by amx_attn_iface.hpp and so by most of tron. No -Werror is set, so this is warning noise, not a build break. The existing forward declarations in h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:17-22 use struct and are the pattern to copy. bf16 is already complete wherever amx_attn_iface.hpp is included, because that header includes the intrinsics-free common/numerics/bf16.hpp, so a bf16 forward declaration adds nothing. The writable native_k_view alias in the same block has no parent caller.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
The new declarations header also forward-declares the actual bf16, view, and const_view classes in their existing namespaces. Declare all five view parameters without defaults.

CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:608-611 (-Wall;-Wpedantic;-Wextra); git grep -i werror at 2880c3aa9b: only GNUmakefile:704 (clang-format --dry-run -Werror); flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:589-594 and nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix@2880c3aa9b:3,13 (clang 19). Minor G09. Type constraints must be body static_asserts, not requires-clauses

Where
5. Interface design (declarations header, design-r3.txt:232-234, :240; build boundaries, :283); 7. Child storage and access (:399-401)
Earlier ids
touches N14 N03
Claim
The design states type constraints for the new owners and views (even rows, columns divisible by chunk_size, T is bf16 or const bf16, a 16,384-byte packed-K owner) but never says how they are expressed. The natural C++20 form, a requires-clause on the class template, breaks two other rules of the same design. First, kv_cache_fwd.hpp forward-declares the five templates without constraints and may include only standard headers, so it cannot name chunk_size, which is defined only in the intrinsics header tron/simd/auto.hpp; a definition with a requires-clause would then be a redeclaration with a different template-head, which is ill-formed. Second, the child's std::conditional_t names k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size> for every head size the book declares (64, 128, 256), while k_vnni::layout_on is true only for 128; if k_vnni_tensor carries a class constraint such as Cols == 128, naming it with a non-dependent head size 64 is ill-formed even in the unselected branch. The reviewer's sketch uses static_asserts inside the class bodies for exactly these checks.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Type constraints are positive even rows and positive columns divisible by chunk_size. Keep stricter operation constraints, including the packed append width. Minor G10. Five view members required 'by expression consumers' that no consumer reads; valid() implies a null view the design does not provide

Where
5. Interface design, Owner and expression reads (design-r3.txt:242)
Claim
The design orders dim, element, const_element, shape0() and valid() on the packed view 'as required by expression consumers', but no expression consumer in the repository reads any of them. The only named consumer, host-tensor construction, uses operator[] alone. valid() additionally implies a default-constructed (null) view, which the design never provides, since it forbids a public raw-pointer constructor and says rows are constructed only by the matrix view. The round-2 text said 'Keep ... consistent with existing views'; round 3 turned that into a requirement claim.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Supply dim, element, const_element, shape0(), and valid() as required by expression consumers. Leave the rank-1 expression concept unchanged. The named V expression consumer is the ordinary host-tensor construction check in section 8.
h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:268-274 (tensor(expr<B,d0,d1,...> const& rhs) loops std::construct_at(&data[i], rhs[i]) and uses nothing else); h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:68-74 (data[i] = other[i] only); h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:147-170 (v_in[i + offset0] and an optional .mat member only). Search of h/tron/tensor, h/tron/kernels and h/tron/hardware for ::dim, ::element, ::const_element and .valid(): only h/tron/hardware/permuted.hpp:120-121, a local alias view<float>::dim, not an expression parameter. shape0() readers: views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:250-253 (array_views, needs views<> inheritance, which design-r3.txt:241 forbids) and hardware/tensor.hpp:486-490 (matmul activations), neither a V consumer. view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:56-58 (valid() means data != nullptr) and :43 (explicit constexpr view(T* data = nullptr)); self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1775, :1810 (only cache use: a default-constructed K view). design-r3.txt:248 (no public raw-pointer constructor, no default constructor). evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:213 (round-2 wording).
Correction from verification

The rank-1 host tensor built from each row reads rhs.chunk(i) and, when N % 16 != 0, rhs.last() [tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:79-85]; the design already specifies chunk and bf16_chunk for the row [design-r3.txt:245-246], and every supported head size (64, 128, 256, 512) is a multiple of 16, so last() is not needed. (3) Citation: the rank-2 tensor constructor spans tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:270-275 (template line 270, signature 272, loop 273-274), not 268-274. (4) The valid() reasoning: the design does not forbid a default constructor; it does not specify one. Replace "which the design never provides, since it forbids a public raw-pointer constructor" with "which the design does not specify: the no-raw-pointer rule at design-r3.txt:248 does not exclude a default constructor, but no sentence provides one, so valid() has no defined false state". Minor G12. bf16_chunk override and decltype test protect a test-only path; the decltype check belongs in the header, not the suite

Where
5. Interface design, operation table (design-r3.txt:247); 8. Behavior checks, Expression reads row (:440)
Earlier ids
touches N13
Claim
The design admits the expression base's bf16_chunk fallback is bit-exact and that only a test constructs a host tensor from the packed view. The mandated override therefore speeds up a path with no production consumer. The round-3 text then adds a decltype assertion inside the test suite to prove the override exists. That is an assertion about structure with no consumer or failure mode, which the repository's test rules forbid, and it differs from what N13 asked for (a static_assert in the header or an instruction record).
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Check decltype(&Row::bf16_chunk) against bf16s (Row::*)(size_t) const noexcept to distinguish the derived override from an inherited fallback. Use an existing hardware-tensor fixture only if it already accepts this input. | Broken expression compatibility or a missing direct-bit override. Bit patterns alone cannot detect the bit-exact fallback.
design-r3.txt:242 ('The named V expression consumer is the ordinary host-tensor construction check in section 8. Production V paths continue to use bulk operations.'), :440. h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:110-117 (if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk) ... else fp32s_to_bf16s(self().chunk(i))). h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp@2880c3aa9b:92-108 (fp32s_to_bf16s changes bits only where the exponent field is 0x7f800000 and the low 16 bits are nonzero); kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2000-2007 (even_part shift and odd_part mask leave the low 16 bits zero), so the fallback is bit-exact; the round-2 N13 lead note confirms it. AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:35-41 ('Do not use tests merely to document the current implementation. Assertions about names, values, defaults, ordering, fields, or structure require an identifiable consumer, user-visible behavior, compatibility requirement, or failure mode'). r2-findings.md N13 recommendation: static_assert(std::is_same_v<decltype(&v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::bf16_chunk), bf16s (v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::*)(size_t) const noexcept>) in the header, or the instruction record, as the detection. design-wdiff.txt:1600 (decltype sentence new in round 3).
Correction from verification
Three narrowings; the substance and both options stand. (a) Replace "which the repository's test rules forbid" with "which the repository's test rules allow only with an identifiable consumer, compatibility requirement, or failure mode [AGENTS.md:35-38@2880c3aa9b]; the design names no production consumer of the bf16 read [design-r3.txt:242], so the check protects only the speed of a test-only read". (b) Add the fact that makes the point sharper: the rest of the row never exercises bf16_chunk at all. The host tensor<N> constructs and assigns from an expression through store_chunk(i, rhs.chunk(i)), the fp32 path [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:80-84, 119-124@2880c3aa9b]; only dmatensor<bf16> calls rhs.bf16_chunk(i) [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:272-276@2880c3aa9b], and the row makes that fixture optional ("only if it already accepts this input", design-r3.txt:440). So the decltype sentence is the row's only bf16_chunk detection, and the "exact bf16 copies" check passes with or without the override. (c) Add that N13's alternative detector is also absent: the instruction-comparison list at design-r3.txt:486-488 does not name the bf16 row read, so the design provides neither the header static_assert nor the instruction record N13 asked for. For Option B, spell the header as h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp (the design's V header, design-r3.txt:372), not vnni.hpp. For Option A, also note that design-r3.txt:328 ("bf16_chunk() preserves stored bits") stays true under the fallback and needs no change, and that section-9 rows :517 and :542 ("Correct override/fallback behavior", "Correct bf16 override detection") should then say the override was dropped rather than corrected.
Narrow three parts of the finding. (1) Replace 'speeds up a path with no production consumer' with 'speeds up a path that nothing in the parent's test plan calls: the named host-tensor consumer reads through chunk(i) only [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:81-83, 272-274], and the only bf16_chunk callers at main are the DMA hardware tensor [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:276, 296, 316, 329] and slice_expr [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:715-716], of which the design makes the hardware-tensor fixture optional [design-r3.txt:440]'. (2) Replace 'which the repository's test rules forbid' with 'which the repository's test rules allow only with an identifiable consumer or a failure mode important to preserve [AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:35-38]; the design's own text removes the failure mode by calling the fallback bit-exact [design-r3.txt:247, :440], and a header static_assert would sit outside the test policy'. (3) Replace 'adds a decltype assertion inside the test suite' with 'does not say where the member-pointer check lives: section 5 says only "Verify the member-pointer type" [design-r3.txt:247], and section 8 lists the decltype check as evidence from the existing tensor/cache cases [design-r3.txt:440], which reads as a suite assertion; round-2 N13 asked for a static_assert in the header'. Keep both options. Add to Option A the precedent that the ordinary contiguous bf16 view defines no bf16_chunk override at main (git grep bf16_chunk at 2880c3aa9b over h/, src/, t/ finds definitions only in dmatensor.hpp:367, permuted.hpp:53, expr.hpp:111/322/715). Add to Option B the cost sentence the design dropped from N13: 'The override saves a shift, a NaN check, and a narrow per chunk; the fallback is bit-exact.' Also note this is a new round-3 simplicity finding, not an N13 residual: N13 asked for the override and the check, and N52 said to keep the row's bf16_chunk body [r2-findings.md N13, N52].
Narrow three points and add one fact. (1) Replace "which the repository's test rules forbid" with "which is hard to justify under AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:35-38: structural assertions need an identifiable consumer or a failure mode that matters, and the only failure mode here is a slower read on a path the design itself calls test-only [design-r3.txt:242]; the suite does hold type-level assertions elsewhere [t/t_fp32.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1329-1342; t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:333-337]". (2) Add: "The design's named consumer does not call bf16_chunk at all. Host tensor construction from an expression calls chunk(i) [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:83,127]. Only dmatensor assignment, permuted.hpp, and expression slices call bf16_chunk [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:276-329; h/tron/hardware/permuted.hpp@2880c3aa9b:53; h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:715-716], and the design makes the hardware-tensor fixture conditional [design-r3.txt:440]. Today's scaled_v_expr declares no bf16_chunk override (no match in kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b)." (3) Replace "adds a decltype assertion inside the test suite" with "places the decltype check only in the section-8 test table [design-r3.txt:440] and gives no location in section 5 [design-r3.txt:247]; the design never writes static_assert or names v_vnni.hpp as the home of the check". (4) In the recommendation, prefer Option B because N13's corrected recommendation kept the override and jhan told codex to follow the corrections; Option A contradicts that recommendation and should be labeled as a departure the design must justify. Disposition of N13: partly (mechanism and bit-exactness corrected; detection is the right kind; header location and objdump region missing).
Recommendation
Pick one and say so. Option A (smallest): in design-r3.txt:247 replace the row with 'v_vnni_row does not override bf16_chunk. Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: the repository rule (AGENTS.md:35-38) allows structural assertions with an identifiable consumer or failure mode rather than forbidding them; the design's named consumer never calls bf16_chunk (host tensors read rows through chunk(i), tensor.hpp:81-83), so the decltype check in the suite protects a path nothing in the parent's test plan exercises. Either option in the recommendation closes it.

Minor G13. even_part and odd_part are private members of scaled_v_expr; the row's chunk() cannot reuse them as written

Where
5. Interface design, pair chunk paragraph (design-r3.txt:251)
Earlier ids
touches N52
Claim
even_part and odd_part are private member functions of detail::scaled_v_expr in kv_cache.hpp, and odd_part reads the member hi_16_of_32. kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp, not the reverse, so v_vnni_row::chunk() in v_vnni.hpp cannot call them. The round-3 edit changed 'Reuse this extraction pattern' to naming the two functions, and does not say whether to hoist them into v_vnni.hpp or copy the two intrinsics. For bf16_chunk the closer existing template is get_v(bf16*)'s cvtepi32_epi16 narrowing, which the design does not point at.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Reuse the existing even_part left shift and odd_part high-half mask for fp32 reads. Narrow the selected halves for bf16 reads.
h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1995-2005 (inside struct scaled_v_expr: private: const __m512i hi_16_of_32; fp32x16 odd_part(bf16x32 x) const noexcept { return _mm512_castsi512_ps(_mm512_and_epi32(x, hi_16_of_32)); } fp32x16 even_part(bf16x32 x) const noexcept { return _mm512_castsi512_ps(_mm512_slli_epi32(x, 16)); }); :2452-2463 (get_v bf16: _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16(both) and _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16(_mm512_srli_epi32(both, 16))). design-r3.txt:284 ('v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions') and :373 (kv_cache.hpp is the includer). evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:225 ('Reuse this extraction pattern'); design-wdiff.txt segments @old 2663-2686.

The defect is that it points at scaled_v_expr's private helpers [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1996-2007] instead of the non-private, identical extraction in page::get_v(float*) [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2416-2431], whose loops the design already moves into v_vnni.hpp as load_row [design-r3.txt:259]. Replace the recommendation with: "Replace the two sentences at design-r3.txt:251 with: 'row.chunk(i) reuses one iteration of the load_row loop moved from get_v(float*) (kv_cache.hpp:2416-2431: _mm512_slli_epi32 by 16 for an even token, _mm512_and_si512 with 0xffff0000 for an odd token). bf16_chunk(i) reuses one iteration of the load_row loop moved from get_v(bf16*) (kv_cache.hpp:2452-2463: _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 after the shift or the srli). scaled_v_expr's private even_part and odd_part (kv_cache.hpp:1996-2007) stay in kv_cache.hpp; kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp, not the reverse.'" Minor G15. fill_random is called a 'poison fill'; it writes finite normal-distribution values

Where
5. Existing callers table, book::fill_random row (design-r3.txt:274); 8. Storage and lifecycle row (:441); context :262
Earlier ids
touches M02
Claim
The new fill_random row tells the implementer to keep the function's 'poison-fill behavior' and section 8 lists 'poison fill' among the lifecycle cases. book::fill_random draws every element from std::normal_distribution<>(0.0, 1.0) through fill_storage_slot and casts each double to bf16. No NaN is written. The design uses 'poison' for NaN everywhere else (:300, :416, :436), and the NaN poison in the existing tests is written through page::set_v with a quiet_NaN row, not through fill_random. The word 'poison' for this fill is new in round 3. An implementer may add NaN filling to fill_random, look for a poison constant, or expect NaN from it.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
| book::fill_random , fill_storage_slot | Preserve the layout-blind whole-block fill as the explicit test-support exception above. | Keep its poison-fill behavior and existing lifecycle-test callers. Do not expose a new public pointer. |
kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1054-1065 (comment 'Fill all kv_block data with random values', 'bypasses the zero-init V slot invariant'; :1059 std::normal_distribution<> dis(0.0, 1.0); for_each_active_slot(... fill_storage_slot<geometry>(kv_storage_offset(slot), dis, gen))); :1365-1375 (data[i] = static_cast<bf16>(distribution(gen))). t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:784-785 and :2008 (the only fill_random callers); :1919-1925, :2701-2705, :2788-2792 (NaN poison via set_v with a quiet_NaN buffer). design-r3.txt:262 (correct description: 'fills an entire K/V block without interpreting coordinates'), :300, :416, :436, :441. design-wdiff.txt:883 (INSERT @old 3183 / new 4305: the row and 'poison-fill' are NEW). Round-2 text mentions 'poison' only at design-revised-text.txt:285, :395, :473, none about fill_random; M02's recommendation said 'byte fill of the whole trivially copyable block'.
Correction from verification
Three narrowings, none changing the verdict. (1) The recommended replacement wording "garbage that bypasses partner zeroing and must not be read" is wrong for one caller. TEST_CASE "apply and join page ranges" [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1953] calls bk.fill_random(gen) at :2008 and then reads the filled values back through page->k(...) and page->get_v(...) into random_ks/random_vs "for emulation::compute_attention" [:2026-2041]. The random values are the test's ordinary K/V input, not unread garbage. Reword to: "Keep its random finite-value fill (std::normal_distribution, kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1059-1061, 1365-1374). It is ordinary random test data that the tests read back through the typed accessors. It bypasses partner zeroing and must not establish readiness. Keep its two callers: TEST_CASE 'fixed KV layout addresses each slot geometry' (:761, calls at :784-785) and TEST_CASE 'apply and join page ranges' (:1953, call at :2008)." (2) "The design uses 'poison' for NaN everywhere else" overstates :416. That sentence refers to t_k_vnni_layout, whose poison is the 0xA5A5 sentinel [t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:51, :108, :191] plus the NaN 0x7FC1 sentinel [:257, :287]. Reword to "a deliberate sentinel bit pattern (NaN at :300 and :436; 0xA5A5 or NaN 0x7FC1 at :416)". A random normal-distribution fill is neither. (3) "existing lifecycle-test callers" in the design row is also loose: neither caller is a lifecycle test. Their tags are [kv_data][book append] (:761) and [page_range] (:1953). For the section-8 row [design-r3.txt:441], keep the finding's hedge: "poison fill" there may mean the NaN set_v sections of TEST_CASE "scaled_v padding NaN" [:2658, sections "append_v preserves poison" :2670 and "single-token dirty-buffer reuse" :2779] rather than fill_random. Ask the design to name which cases it means.

In loose engineering usage "poison" can mean any garbage sentinel, and the operation is a fill with values that must not be read; the defect is that the design's own three uses [design-r3.txt:300, :416, :436] and the existing tests [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1919-1925, :2696-2705, :2785-2789] all mean NaN, and the Words table [:8] defines NaN but not "poison". Minor G16. No stated forward-declaration path for k_vnni_view in the intrinsics-free kernel interface header

Where
5. Build and include boundaries (design-r3.txt:284); 7. Child adaptation (:391-392) and Copy and score readers row (:407)
Earlier ids
touches N58 N03
Claim
The child's typed kernel declaration qk_vnni_128x4(k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128> k_page, ...) lives in h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp, which the design keeps free of intrinsics. A by-value parameter in a declaration needs at least a declaration of the class template. The parent's kv_cache_fwd.hpp declares only the V types and the native-K aliases. The design defines k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view and k_vnni_row in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp, which also receives the AVX-512 scatter, gather and block-store bodies, so the interface header cannot include it. The design never says that the child adds the K forward declarations to kv_cache_fwd.hpp, or where else amx_attn_iface.hpp gets them. An implementer must guess the place or include the intrinsics header.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
amx_attn_iface.hpp includes kv_cache_fwd.hpp and remains free of intrinsics. v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions. [...] Define k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and k_vnni_row for the proposed logical expression reads in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp.
h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp@30c4ac82cb:251-254 (void qk_vnni_128x4(const bf16* k_vnni_plane, const bf16* q_rows, float* s, size_t s_stride_floats) noexcept); @2880c3aa9b:1-5 (header rule: no intrinsics). h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@30c4ac82cb:21 (#include "tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp" // intrinsics-free). h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:35 (#include <immintrin.h>), :139-143 (scatter_row uses _mm512_loadu_si512 and _mm512_i32scatter_epi32), :193-197 (store_block). Fake definitions needing the complete type: t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@30c4ac82cb:196; real definition src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@30c4ac82cb:201. design-r3.txt:232 (parent kv_cache_fwd.hpp contents: no packed-K types), :284, :390, :391-392, :405-407.

Replace the clause with the stronger reason: k_vnni_view and k_vnni_row are expression types [design-r3.txt:391, and :244 for the V parallel], so their defining header includes h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp, which includes tron/simd/auto.hpp, which includes <immintrin.h> [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:8; h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@2880c3aa9b:6]. Minor G17. Header home of the child's typed K storage operations and of detail::k_vnni_access is not stated; 'k_vnni.hpp' is named without a path

Where
7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:390-392); 5. Internal storage access row (:249) and Build and include boundaries (:284); Q1 (:95)
Earlier ids
touches N58 N03 M07 N59
Claim
The round-2 text placed 'the K mapping and storage operations' in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp. The round-3 text assigns only the three types, the mapping constants, layout_on and index to that header, and says the kernel header 'retains software score arithmetic'. The typed store_row, load_row, store_block, token copy and plane copy that rows 405-407 call are assigned to no header. The friend accessor detail::k_vnni_access is placed 'in its own header', where 'its' can mean the child or the struct. If an implementer keeps scatter_row, gather_row and store_block in h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp (their location today), kv_cache.hpp must keep including the kernel header, which the round-2 split was meant to end, and 'The cache includes the K tensor header' is not sufficient. After the port two headers share the basename k_vnni.hpp; line 284 names 'k_vnni.hpp' without a path. Line 95 says 'Keep the shared access plumbing small' while line 249 defines no shared access struct.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Define k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and k_vnni_row for the proposed logical expression reads in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp. Move mapping constants, k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>, and the existing constexpr free function k_vnni::index there. [...] The cache includes the K tensor header. h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp includes that header and retains software score arithmetic. [...] The child uses a separate detail::k_vnni_access in its own header. [...] v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions.

Minor G19. for_each_active_slot visits logical slots, not physical; 'logical alias' is undefined and cannot occur in a fixed layout

Where
6. Arena construction without clearing, heterogeneous bullet (design-r3.txt:350)
Earlier ids
touches M04 N25
Claim
The heterogeneous bullet says for_each_active_slot 'dispatches the physical slot to its compile-time geometry' and ends with a caution about a 'logical alias' that the design never defines. In the code, for_each_active_slot iterates slot indices into kv_slot_specs and keeps only slot < logical_slot_count(); its comment calls these logical slots; callers form the physical offset with kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does. A fixed (heterogeneous) layout maps each logical slot to exactly one physical offset: make_kv_slot_offsets computes one prefix per slot and storage_location reads offsets.values[offset.i]. The only aliasing mechanism in book is storage_view_offset_, set by set_storage_view (the EAGLE view shift), read only in the uniform accessor; EAGLE requires uniform geometry, so it never enters the heterogeneous enumeration. For logical slots the two numbers coincide, so the effect is unchanged, but the bullet opens with 'enumerate physical storage offsets', so a reader may look for a physical-offset loop the helper does not provide, or add a deduplication structure for a case that cannot occur. The alias sentence is carried over from round 2 and no earlier round raised it.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which dispatches the physical slot to its compile-time geometry. ... Do not construct the same region again for a logical alias.
kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1243-1249 (for_each_active_slot: if (slot < active_slots) function.template operator()<slot, geometry>()), :918-922 (comment 'logical K/V slots'), :1060-1061 (fill_random forms kv_storage_offset(slot)), :654-670 (make_kv_slot_offsets: per-slot prefix sums, one arena_offset per slot), :727-735 (storage_location: offsets.values[offset.i]; slot_specs[offset.i] at :732), :892-901 (set_storage_view writes storage_view_offset_), :1286-1294 (storage_view_offset_ used only inside the uniform kv_block accessor), :771 (eagle_compatible requires layout_t::uniform_geometry), :1158-1159 (the only extra physical slot, EAGLE, cannot exist in a heterogeneous layout). design-wdiff.txt:1071 ('again for a logical alias' is OLD context). grep of r1-findings.md and r2-findings.md for 'logical alias': 0 hits. design-r3.txt:348, :350, :521 (section 9 N25 M04 row), S16 links (design-r3-links.tsv:200-201).
Correction from verification
Keep the finding as minor with four precision edits. (a) 'read only in the uniform accessor': storage_view_offset_ is also read in refresh_kv_blocks_alias (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1222-1227), which derives kv_blocks_alias for the uniform_reclaimability branch of the same accessor (:1284-1289); cite :1222-1227 and :1277-1300 together. The conclusion (only the requires(uniform_geometry) accessor is shifted; kv_block_at_storage<geometry> at :1309-1335 is not) stands. (b) 'its comment calls these logical slots': for_each_active_slot has no comment; the phrase 'logical K/V slots' is the logical_slot_count() comment (:918-919). Reword to 'the logical_slot_count() comment (:918-919) names them logical K/V slots'. (c) 'A fixed (heterogeneous) layout': heterogeneous_kv_cache is defined with fixed_kv_layout (heterogeneous_kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:36-37; allow_layer_prefix = false at kv_cache.hpp:745), so the wording is right; note that the one-slot-one-offset property holds for every kv_layout because make_kv_slot_offsets (:651-670) runs per slot regardless of allow_layer_prefix. (d) Add the one plausible intended meaning and dismiss it: Note [KV slot retention] (:113-115) says several operations can consume one slot, and the Words table (design-r3.txt:32) says a slot may be 'shared by several layers'; a per-slot loop visits such a slot once, so the caution is needless under that reading too. Recommendation addition: also define in the Words table 'logical slot = an index into slot_specs, 0 to logical_slot_count()-1 (kv_cache.hpp:918-922); storage offset = kv_storage_offset, equal to the logical index for logical slots, plus the EAGLE extra slot at logical_slot_count() (:906-907)', and delete the alias sentence or fold it into the next bullet (design-r3.txt:351), which already forbids reconstructing the retained EAGLE slot. Note for the section-9 row 'N25 M04' (design-r3.txt:521): the row says 'Use existing geometry dispatch and filter by arena'; the body adopted M04's helper name and filter but not the corrected recommendation's 'as fill_random does' and 'at slot_base(kv_storage_offset(slot))' clauses (r2-findings.md '### M04', design-text lens correction (d)), which is the gap this finding fills.
Three narrowings; the verdict and the recommended replacement text stand. (a) Narrow "cannot occur in a fixed layout". Sharing does exist in fixed layouts, one level above slots: the design's own Words table says "A slot is a cache region used by one layer or shared by several layers" [design-r3.txt:32], and Note [KV slot retention] says "Several operations can consume one slot" [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:113-116]. That is a layer-to-slot or operation-to-slot mapping. for_each_active_slot iterates slot indices [:1243-1249], not layers or operations, so it visits each slot once. Restate the claim as: "no two slot indices of the enumeration map to one physical region, and layer-to-slot sharing happens above the slot index, so it cannot cause double construction in a slot-indexed loop." (b) Fix the storage_view_offset_ citation. It is not "used only inside the uniform kv_block accessor". It is also read by refresh_kv_blocks_alias [:1224, :1227], which sets kv_blocks_alias, and kv_blocks_alias is read by the uniform_reclaimability branch of the uniform accessor [:1285-1288]. The substantive point survives: kv_block_at_storage [:1311-1336], the path the heterogeneous construction and fill_storage_slot [:1366-1373] use, calls slot_base(offset) directly and never consults storage_view_offset_. (c) Add one fact to the evidence: the round-2 M04 recommendation (which section 9 row "N25 M04" [design-r3.txt:521] claims to have applied) said "for each active logical slot, as fill_random does"; the design's "dispatches the physical slot" is a departure from that corrected text, so this is a small slip introduced by the round-2 fix, not an error inherited unchanged. Recommended text (unchanged in substance, one citation added): "Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which calls the lambda once per active logical slot index with that slot's compile-time geometry (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1249). Form the storage offset as kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does (:1060-1061). Filter on layout_t::storage_location(kv_storage_offset(slot), logical_slot_count()).reclaimable. A fixed layout maps each slot index to one distinct physical region (make_kv_slot_offsets :654-670), so no deduplication is needed." Delete "Do not construct the same region again for a logical alias." The EAGLE bullet [design-r3.txt:351] already carries the only real double-construction caution and needs no change.
Keep the claim and recommendation. Narrow two evidence citations: (a) the "logical K/V slots" comment is at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:917 with the function at :918-921, not :918-922; (b) storage_view_offset_ is not "read only in the uniform accessor": refresh_kv_blocks_alias also reads it [:1222-1227] to set kv_blocks_alias [:1128], which the uniform_reclaimability branch of the accessor then reads [:1285-1288]. The accessor itself carries requires(layout_t::uniform_geometry) [:1276-1277], so the substance (the EAGLE view shift touches only uniform-geometry access) stands. Add to the claim that M04's corrected recommendation asked for the words "each active logical slot" and the design substituted "physical slot", so this is a small deviation from the corrected M04 text, and that the same bullet group uses "physical KV slot" for the EAGLE slot [design-r3.txt:351], which is the code's meaning of physical (kv_storage_offset beyond the logical count). Recommended replacement text is acceptable as written; add one clause so the implementer keeps the arena filter and the EAGLE bullet unchanged: "Keep the storage_location(...).reclaimable filter and the following EAGLE bullet as they are."
Recommendation
Replace the two sentences with: 'Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which calls the lambda once per active logical slot index with that slot's compile-time geometry (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1249). Form the physical offset as kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does (:1060-1061). A fixed layout maps each logical slot to one distinct physical region, so no deduplication is needed.'
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3. This is the loose fact inside the M04 fix, so M04 is graded partly this round. Corrections: storage_view_offset_ is also read by refresh_kv_blocks_alias (kv_cache.hpp:1222-1227), not only by the uniform accessor; layer-to-slot sharing exists one level above slot indices, so say "no two slot indices map to one physical region" rather than "no aliasing". M04's corrected recommendation asked for "each active logical slot" and the design wrote "physical slot".

Minor G21. Adding reinterpret_cast to the rg completion search makes it unusable

Where
7. Implementation steps, caller search (design-r3.txt:380-382)
Earlier ids
touches M02 M07
Claim
Round 3 added reinterpret_cast to the search pattern that the implementer runs over h, src and t before and after the accessor migration. The design says the second run 'checks for obsolete calls and comments'. At round-2 main that one alternative alone matches 979 lines in 74 files across the whole tree. The other alternatives match a few dozen lines. The check therefore stops being a completion test unless the implementer narrows it on their own.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
rg -n "v_base|v_data|set_v|get_v|append_v|scaled_v|fill_storage_slot|fill_random|reinterpret_cast|qk_rowmajor_128x4|weights_times_v_128x4" h src t
git grep -c reinterpret_cast 2880c3aa9b -- h src t, summed: 979 matching lines in 74 files. design-wdiff.txt: REPLACE @old 4685 / new 6118 added fill_storage_slot|fill_random|reinterpret_cast to the pattern (the round-2 pattern had none of the three). design-r3.txt:380 ('The second search checks for obsolete calls and comments').
Correction from verification
Narrow two statements and refine the recommendation. (a) Replace "The other alternatives match a few dozen lines" with measured numbers at 2880c3aa9b over h, src and t: without reinterpret_cast the pattern matches 514 lines in 74 files (get_v 136 lines, of which 122 are get_vocab_size, get_value, get_vocabulary_data and similar substrings, not get_v calls; set_v 112; v_base 82; v_data 79; scaled_v 70; fill_random 12; append_v 10; qk_rowmajor_128x4 9; weights_times_v_128x4 7; fill_storage_slot 3). With reinterpret_cast the whole pattern matches 1491 lines in 124 files. So reinterpret_cast supplies 979 of 1491 lines (66 percent) and adds 50 files that no other alternative touches, such as t/t_hwattention.cpp (17 lines) and t/t_mwaitx.cpp (12 lines). (b) Replace "stops being a completion test" with "was never a zero-hit test and now triples in size". The pattern keeps fill_random and fill_storage_slot, which the design says to preserve [design-r3.txt:262, 274], so a non-empty result is expected even after a correct migration. The defect is that the design does not say what the implementer should look for among the reinterpret_cast hits. (c) Refine the scoped command with the expected residue. At 2880c3aa9b, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp has 27 reinterpret_cast lines. 17 touch the V plane and should disappear or move into typed operations: 1371 (fill_storage_slot, kept as the documented exception), 1681, 1689, 1706, 1716 (v_data forms), 1878, 1881, 1950, 1953 (V token copies), 2239 (v_base), 2296, 2335, 2376, 2414, 2448 (set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v), 2455, 2463 (scaled_v stores). 10 are K-plane, block-array or EAGLE casts that stay: 1288, 1297, 1333 (kv_block_t arrays), 1749, 1756 (EAGLE x), 2017, 2086, 2101 (bf16x32 K reads), 2211, 2218 (K views). The 5 casts in h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp (594, 598, 655, 656, 869), the 1 in h/tron/models/model.hpp (1185) and the 2 in t/t_llama_unit.cpp (2363, 2382) are query, hardware or device casts, not V-plane casts. So the design should say: "rg -n reinterpret_cast h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp; after migration the only V-plane casts left are inside the typed operations and the fill_storage_slot exception; the K-plane, block-array and EAGLE casts remain." (d) Note on the recommendation's "Keep fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern": the round-2 design-text-lens correction to M02 said not to add those names to the pattern [r2-findings.md, M02 design-text-lens correction]. Either choice costs at most 15 lines of output, so this does not affect the verdict, but the finding should not present keeping them as required.
Narrow the claim to: "Round 3 added reinterpret_cast to the search pattern without a purpose or a file scope. At 2880c3aa9b that alternative adds 979 lines in 74 files (h, src, t). The rest of the pattern already matched 514 lines in 74 files through substring hits such as get_vocab_size and set_valid. With reinterpret_cast the output is 1491 lines in 124 files, and 944 of the added lines are in 70 files the parent does not change. The design never says what the reinterpret_cast hits are for, and its definition-of-done rule about packed physical indexing [design-r3.txt:495] names no command that checks it." Drop the phrases "The other alternatives match a few dozen lines" and "stops being a completion test": the search was a review list, not a zero-hit test, before and after the round-3 edit [design-r3.txt:380; expected surviving names at :270-271, :274-276, :278]. Keep the recommendation to leave fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern. Reword the second command so it covers every parent file that holds a cast today and states the expected survivors: rg -n reinterpret_cast h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp h/tron/models/model.hpp t/t_llama_unit.cpp with the sentence "After migration the only V-plane casts must be inside v_vnni.hpp's typed operations and in book::fill_storage_slot (the documented exception at design-r3.txt:262). Casts of the K plane, of the block array on allocation/restore, and the non-cache casts in self_attention.hpp:594-656, model.hpp:1185 and t_llama_unit.cpp:2363/2382 (all at 2880c3aa9b) are expected to remain." The finding's two-file scope (kv_cache.hpp + v_vnni.hpp) is sufficient for V-plane casts at 2880c3aa9b, because none of the 8 casts in the other parent files touches V (self_attention.hpp:594/598/655/656 wrap Q rows and activations, :869 an fp32 hardware head, model.hpp:1185 a uint16 view of an array, t_llama_unit.cpp:2363/2382 a PosDevice pointer); naming the parent's files anyway keeps the command valid if step 4 moves a V cast into the kernel call sites.
Narrow three parts. (1) Replace "The other alternatives match a few dozen lines" with measured numbers: at 2880c3aa9b the round-2 pattern already matched 499 lines in 71 files, because several names are substrings (get_v 136 lines, 22 whole-word; set_v 112/38; v_data 79/23; v_base 82/61; scaled_v 70/40); the round-3 pattern matches 1491 lines in 124 files, so reinterpret_cast triples the lines and adds 53 files. (3) Fix the recommendation's completion criterion. "The only V-plane casts left must be inside the typed operations and the fill_storage_slot exception" is too strict: the design keeps the 8-lane page::v views (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1681, 1689, 1706, 1716, inside the #else at :1674-1725) and the 8-lane copy_storage_slot V memcpy (:1950, :1953, inside the #else at :1947-1956) [design-r3.txt:271, :273], plus the K-plane k_view casts (:2211, :2218), the EAGLE x() casts (:1749, :1756) and the block-array casts (:1288, :1297, :1333). Keep fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern only if the design wants them there; note that the M02 design-text-lens correction said not to add them, which is a M02 grading matter, not this finding.
Recommendation
Keep fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern. Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3. Minor G22. The t_amx_numerics PV case cannot keep its hand-packed std::vector V page; no permitted typed route is named

Where
7. Implementation steps, step 6 (design-r3.txt:377); 5. Interface design, view rules (:248-249) and kernel row (:275, :277); 6. Arena construction (:352)
Earlier ids
touches N22 N15
Claim
The PV numerics case builds its V page by hand in a std::vector<tron::bf16> with the interleave formula and passes v_page.data() to weights_times_v_128x4. With the typed signature (:277) it must pass a v_vnni_view<const bf16, 64, 128>. The design forbids a public raw-pointer constructor (:248), forbids tests from using detail::v_vnni_access (:249), and forbids casting existing storage into the owner (:352). Step 6 says only 'explicit aligned row wrappers', which fits the K array (an explicit const_native_k_view wrap is legal per :267) but not V. The implementing agent at t_amx_numerics.cpp:226 cannot tell which permitted route to take (an owner filled by 64 append_v_row calls, an owner filled by element writes, or a memcpy of the hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner) or what to do with the test's own interleave formula, which then duplicates production layout knowledge. Round-2 N22 covered only the K array of this test.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
| 6. Existing tests and fixtures | t/t_llama_unit.cpp , t/t_amx_numerics.cpp , t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp , t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp | Update real/fake signatures, explicit aligned row wrappers, and the typed-access concept probe. Add alignment to the heterogeneous V fixture. Extend existing cases only for uncovered contracts.
t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:198-211 (std::vector<tron::bf16> v_page(PAGE_TOKENS_64 * HEAD_SIZE_128) filled as v_page[(pair * HEAD_SIZE_128 + d) * 2 + j]), :226-227 (weights_times_v_128x4(v_page.data(), &exp_rows[0][0], PAGE_TOKENS_64, o_amx)), :236-239 (reference reads v_page by the same formula), :66 and :158-159 (K array in->k, whose explicit wrap design-r3.txt:267 keeps legal). design-r3.txt:248, :249, :252, :267, :277, :352, :377, :416 (the child's t_k_vnni_layout gets owner instructions: fill with typed row saves, copy the owner's bytes to a test buffer).
Correction from verification
Three narrowings. (a) Reword the reading of step 6: "explicit aligned row wrappers" [design-r3.txt:377] is not K-only. "Explicit ... wrap" is the N22 language for the K plane, and "aligned row wrappers" is the N15 language for append_v_row source rows. The phrase therefore hints at the append_v_row route for V, but step 6 never says that the PV case must replace its hand-packed std::vector page with an owner, which fill route to use, or what happens to the test's own interleave formula and its pair-formula reference. Replace "which fits the K array ... but not V" with "which names a K plane wrap and V source-row wraps but does not say that the PV case's std::vector page itself must move into an owner". (b) Soften the :352 citation: "Do not construct in an accessor or cast an existing SIMD array object into the new owner" [:352] is an arena-construction rule about kv_block's SIMD array; a memcpy of bytes into a trivially copyable owner is not a cast, so the design text leaves the memcpy route unaddressed rather than forbidden. The finding's advice "Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner" stands as a recommendation, not as an existing design rule. (c) Fix two line ranges: the hand-packed fill starts at the comment on :197 (cite :197-211, not :198-211), and the reference read is :238-239 (not :236-239). Optional addition to the recommendation: the reference loop can read the logical float array v through bf16_rne (vv = bf16_to_float(bf16_rne(v[t * HEAD_SIZE_128 + d]))), which needs no layout formula at all; the byte-copy comparison is then the only place the pair formula survives.
Narrow to a note on step 6 [design-r3.txt:377]: "The PV case of t/t_amx_numerics.cpp [2880c3aa9b:203-227] replaces its std::vector<bf16> page with a v_vnni_tensor<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> owner (16,384 bytes, 64-byte aligned, default-constructed). Fill it through the public typed operations, either element writes [design-r3.txt:317] or append_v_row from 64-byte-aligned bf16 rows [design-r3.txt:252-257], and pass owner.as_view() to weights_times_v_128x4. Keep the case's independent physical-layout formula [2880c3aa9b:207, :239] as a check by copying the owner's bytes to a test buffer, as section 7 prescribes for t_k_vnni_layout [design-r3.txt:416]. The QK case keeps its raw K array through an explicit const_native_k_view wrap [design-r3.txt:267]." Drop the citation of design-r3.txt:352 (an arena rule, not a test rule) and drop "Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner" (not a design rule; the design is silent on it). Relates to N22 (K wrap) and N15 (aligned rows); neither asked for this V-test text.
Two narrowings and one softening. (1) The clause "forbids casting existing storage into the owner (:352)" over-reads the text. design-r3.txt:352 says "Do not construct in an accessor or cast an existing SIMD array object into the new owner" and sits in the arena-construction section; it addresses the kv_block SIMD array, not test-owned storage. Reword to: "the design states no rule for test-owned storage; :352 covers only the cache's existing SIMD array." (2) In the recommendation, replace "Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner" with "State whether a memcpy of hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner is allowed in tests. Prefer the append_v_row route, which keeps the pair formula inside the type, and keep any independent formula check on bytes copied OUT of the owner, as :416 prescribes for t_k_vnni_layout." A memcpy into a trivially copyable owner is legal C++, so the design should choose, not the finder. (3) Keep the rest: the evidence lines are right (v_page fill at t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203-211, kernel call :226-227, reference read :239), the owner size 64 x 128 x 2 bytes = 16,384 bytes is right, and an alignas(64) owner works as a local or through std::make_unique (C++17 aligned new). Add to the finding that t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp's fakes (@2880c3aa9b:180-185) need only the signature change, so the PV case is the single test that needs a named V input route.
Recommendation
Add to step 6, or to the 'AMX kernels and test substitutes' row: 'The PV case of t/t_amx_numerics.cpp (:198-227) cannot keep its hand-packed std::vector<bf16> page: the typed view has no pointer route and tests do not use detail::v_vnni_access. Hold the page in a v_vnni_tensor<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> (16,384 bytes; heap-allocated or an aligned local), fill it with 64 append_v_row calls from 64-byte-aligned bf16 rows (the bf16 input path keeps exact bits), pass owner.as_view() to the kernel, and keep the logical V array as the reference. Keep the independent pair-formula check only by copying the owner's bytes into a plain bf16 buffer, as section 7 prescribes for t_k_vnni_layout. Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner. The QK case keeps its raw K array through an explicit const_native_k_view<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> wrap.'
Lead's reading
Holds 2 of 3. The design-text verifier refuted the second half: a permitted route is derivable from the fixture rule at :317 (direct element writes) plus owner.as_view(). The source and intent verifiers hold because the design never says that the PV case's std::vector page itself must move into an owner, which fill route to use, or what happens to the test's own interleave formula. Two judges list this among the items to settle before implementation. Corrections: :352 is an arena rule, not a test rule; a memcpy of hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner is legal C++, so the design should state whether tests may do it rather than the finding forbidding it.
Found by
cpp-interface, handoff; 2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Where

Earlier ids
touches N33
Claim
The design tells the implementer to update 'both exact-call expectations' in ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs. The child spec has two test cases that assert the generated save calls, and each case holds four assertions that change when save_k_helper is merged into save_k. The count of 'outer_state.template save_k<' is asserted to be 1 in both cases; after the merge run() and run_main_help both emit that text, so the count becomes 2. The count of 'outer_state.template save_k_helper<' is asserted to be 1 in both cases and becomes 0. The run() call text ',k,n_workers);' becomes ',k,0,n_workers);'. Only the two exact-call strings are named by the design, and the S18 link covers only the first case (lines 2808-2818). An implementer who follows the sentence literally leaves six failing assertions.
Evidence

Minor G24. Child port says nothing about its test-cost records

Where
7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:386-395); 8. Child only row (:443); 8. Commands and execution hosts (:478)
Claim
The child diff adds a config/test-benchmarks.json record for t_k_vnni_layout (only in the granite_rapids_6962p block) and changes the t_llama_unit record of that block from 3.38 s to 13.436 s at one slice. The repository test skill requires bin/slice bench --new or --update <name> for new or materially changed tests. The design's adaptation of t_k_vnni_layout to typed owners and of the save_k case to the merged save_k materially changes both tests. Section 8 states the bench rule and its authorization for the parent's t_llama_unit only. The child section does not say whether the child's records are carried over, re-measured, or which platform block is affected.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
On delphi-3bda, bin/slice bench --update t_llama_unit refreshes the granite_rapids_6962p platform block only. Name the other platform keys, genoa96, genoa32, and granite_rapids_6960p, as unrefreshed. Bench requests the whole host and measures one, two, and four slices, so it requires user direction.

Neither the child section (:384-427) nor the Child only row (:443) names the child's two records." 2. Add the concrete consequence. "Main's granite_rapids_6962p t_llama_unit row still reads 3.38/3.38/3.64 s [config/test-benchmarks.json@1400fa4481:421]. The parent's refresh (design-r3.txt:478) rewrites that row. The child's diff rewrites the same row to 13.436/13.388/13.618 s [config/test-benchmarks.json@30c4ac82cb:426]. The port therefore conflicts on that row. The conflict bullet at design-r3.txt:389 names only common.hpp and self_attention.hpp." Recommend adding config/test-benchmarks.json to the named conflict files at :389, with the rule "resolve by re-measuring on the ported child, never by keeping either old number." 3. Minor G25. Successful Slice case logs: location, runner, 10-run pruning and fallback are not stated

Where
8. CI ownership and execution evidence (design-r3.txt:469-470); Definition of done (:498)
Earlier ids
touches N36
Claim
The design makes the per-case Catch2 starting and Catch2 passed lines from the Slice logs-*/run-* files the proof that the required Test host job executed the new cases, and says successful logs are not uploaded and must be preserved or linked explicitly. It does not say where those files are (REPO_ROOT/logs-<runner hostname>/run-NNNN/<test>.log on the self-hosted runner), that the GitHub job log for a passing run carries no per-case output (only a final 'Logs: <run_dir>' line), that Slice deletes the oldest run directory once 10 run directories exist, how to identify the runner, where the test-runtime and environment store paths it asks to record can be read, or what to do when the runner is unreachable. An implementing agent who looks for the evidence after a few more CI runs finds the directory gone and has only the green check, the false-evidence case N36 warned about.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Record the required GCP Nix Test host run URL and attempt, source key, test-runtime and environment store paths, case names, and selected command/filter. Preserve the existing Catch2 starting and Catch2 passed lines from the corresponding Slice logs-*/run-* files. These prove which cases executed. Successful logs are not automatically uploaded: the reviewed upload step runs only on failure. Preserve or link successful runner logs explicitly. [S19]
