Minor G26. Definition of done does not carry the test-cost record refresh

Where
8. Definition of done for the parent (design-r3.txt:495-499)
Earlier ids
touches N36 N38 N41
Claim
The definition of done does not name the required CI checks that must be green on the final commit, does not mention the format and lint gate, does not carry the repository's test-cost refresh obligation, and does not state the PR label state. Each is a repository rule the design cites elsewhere, and each is checkable, so leaving them out lets an implementer declare the parent done while a rule is unmet. The refresh obligation matters because CI's cost-data check only reports missing records, so a stale record for a materially changed t_llama_unit does not fail CI and nothing else forces the refresh.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Required tests collect and execute new behavior coverage with the named evidence. AMX-enabled, Debug, and limited 8-lane checks are reported separately. Baseline failures, placeholders, and skipped cases are named explicitly.

Claim

Minor G49. AMX-on row asks for 'evidence that the kernel ran' without naming a method

Where
8. Build configurations table, row '16 lanes, AMX code on' (design-r3.txt:453; rule at :445)
Claim
The row requires 'evidence that the kernel ran' for real numerical results but names no artifact that counts. The design's own rule at :445 says hardware-unavailable early returns do not count as execution. The AMX tests print a WARN line and return when the runtime probe says AMX is unavailable, and the probe also returns false when TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 is set. A concrete, source-backed method exists and should be named; otherwise the implementer may present a green t_amx_numerics run from a host without AMX as kernel execution.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Compile real kernels and unconditional fake definitions. Execute applicable fake dispatch cases. Real numerical results require compatible AMX hardware and evidence that the kernel ran.
t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:146, :190 (WARN("AMX unavailable on this host - QK precision check skipped") and the PV counterpart before returning); t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@30c4ac82cb:199-202 (same pattern for the VNNI QK check). h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp@2880c3aa9b:167-170 and src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@2880c3aa9b:61 (available() true when CPU, OS and per-process permission allow AMX and TRON_AMX_DISABLE is not 1). design-r3.txt:445, :453.
Correction from verification
Narrow the recommendation to t_amx_numerics only. t_amx_dispatch_dtype defines its own fake bool available() noexcept { return true; } and fake kernels, and its header says "no AMX instruction is linked or executed; available() is the fake too" [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:4-8, 166-170]. It therefore never prints the "AMX unavailable" WARN, and TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 has no effect on it. The design row already covers it correctly as "Execute applicable fake dispatch cases" [design-r3.txt:453]. Fix citations: the TRON_AMX_DISABLE read is in detect_and_request [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@2880c3aa9b:58-62], not in available() itself [:76-86]; the child's WARN sites are :146-148 (QK), :199-201 (VNNI QK) and :247-249 (PV) at 30c4ac82cb. Reworded recommendation: after "evidence that the kernel ran" add: "Evidence is the Slice run log (logs-<host>/run-NNNN/t_amx_numerics.log) from delphi-3bda in which the cases 'AMX QK does not digress from the AVX dotter' and 'AMX PV stays inside the AVX weight-rounding envelope' show Catch2 passed and no 'AMX unavailable on this host' WARN line, plus a control run of the same binary with TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 whose log shows those WARN lines. 'Catch2 passed' alone is not enough: the tests WARN and return when available() is false, and that still passes. t_amx_dispatch_dtype uses fake kernels and is not hardware evidence." The parent row can reuse the runtime TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 control the design already prescribes for the child [design-r3.txt:443]. Also add the WARN-line rule as a sentence under :469 so the CI-evidence paragraph and the :445 rule agree.
Narrow the recommendation to t_amx_numerics and drop t_amx_dispatch_dtype. Suggested design text after "evidence that the kernel ran" (design-r3.txt:453): "Evidence is the t_amx_numerics output from delphi-3bda in which the two AMX precision cases (QK vs the AVX dotter; PV weight-rounding envelope) end with Catch2 passed and contain no 'AMX unavailable on this host' WARN line [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:145-147, 189-191], plus a control run of the same binary with TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 whose output shows both WARN lines [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@2880c3aa9b:61-62]. A Catch2 passed line alone is not enough: the listener logs passed whenever no assertion failed, which is also true after the early return [t/testlog.cpp@2880c3aa9b:21-25]. Record both output paths. t_amx_dispatch_dtype is not kernel-ran evidence: it links CPU fakes for every entry point and its fake available() returns true regardless of hardware or TRON_AMX_DISABLE [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:5-8, 168-170]." For the child, add the third case (VNNI QK identity, t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@30c4ac82cb:199-201) to the same rule. Also fix the S13 label at design-r3.txt:577: "[AMX availability check]" is marked "Child:" but the same early return exists on main at 2880c3aa9b:145-147 and 189-191, so the sentence should read "Main and child: the AMX numerical test returns early ...". Whether the Slice log captures the Catch2 reporter's WARN output (as opposed to the test binary's own stdout) was not verified here; the design should say "test output or Slice log" until that is checked.
Narrow the recommendation in three ways. (1) Drop t_amx_dispatch_dtype from the kernel-execution evidence. That test defines available() as a fake returning true and defines all three kernels as CPU fakes; its header says no AMX instruction is linked or executed [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:4-8, 168-170, 174-185]. It has no "AMX unavailable" WARN line. Its passed sections prove fake dispatch, which the row already covers under "Execute applicable fake dispatch cases" [design-r3.txt:453]. (2) Name the artifact precisely: the Slice run log logs-<host>/run-NNNN/t_amx_numerics.log, which holds the test process's stdout and stderr [bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:2770, 3148, 3174, 3182-3187], must contain "Catch2 passed" for "AMX QK does not digress from the AVX dotter" and "AMX PV stays inside the AVX weight-rounding envelope" (plus the child's "VNNI QK identity" case) and must contain no line with "AMX unavailable on this host". State why: "Catch2 passed" alone is printed for the early-return path too [t/testlog.cpp@2880c3aa9b:21-25]. (3) Keep the TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 control run optional. If used, set the variable in the environment before the process starts, because available() reads it once at the first call and caches the result [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp@2880c3aa9b:76-80]. Suggested row text after "evidence that the kernel ran": "Evidence is the Slice run log of t_amx_numerics from delphi-3bda with Catch2 passed lines for the AMX precision cases and no 'AMX unavailable on this host' line [S13 AMX availability check, S19 Case execution records]; a Catch2 passed line alone does not prove execution, because the availability early return also ends with zero failed assertions." Also fix S13: it labels the early return "Child:" [design-r3.txt:577], but the same early returns exist on main [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:145-147, 189-191], so the row applies to the parent as well.
Recommendation
After 'evidence that the kernel ran' add: 'Evidence is the Slice log of t_amx_numerics and t_amx_dispatch_dtype from delphi-3bda showing the AMX precision sections passed with no AMX unavailable on this host WARN line, plus a control run of the same binaries with TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 whose log shows the WARN lines. Record both log paths.'
Lead's reading
The finder graded this a note; all three verifiers grade it minor and the lead follows them. A "Catch2 passed" line proves nothing here: both AMX precision cases return before any assertion when available() is false, after a WARN line, and the listener logs passed whenever no assertion failed (testlog.cpp:21-25). Correction: drop t_amx_dispatch_dtype from the kernel-ran evidence; it links CPU fakes and a fake available() that returns true. Name the t_amx_numerics Slice log with no "AMX unavailable" WARN line plus a TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1 control run.
Found by
process-ci; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor H01. Local gate list omits make lint-notes, which the required Lint job runs

Where
8 Commands and execution hosts, design-r3.txt:477; 6 Copies and hardware preparation, design-r3.txt:362-363
Earlier ids
touches G26, N28, C06
Claim
The design's local check list is make format, make lint, make build-test, make test-host. The required Lint job runs bin/ci/run-lint-checks.sh, which builds the Nix check lint-toolchain, and that check ends with make lint-notes. lint-notes is a separate Make target and is not a dependency of lint. It fails on any 'See Note [X]' whose definition is missing or lacks the '~~~' underline. The parent edits Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots], which eight comments reference (kv_cache.hpp:1056, 1886, 1905, 2197, 2298, 2337, 2378; t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2650), and it relocates layout documentation into v_vnni.hpp with cross-references from four other files. A malformed or renamed Note passes make lint locally and fails the required check. The checker also needs the .#lint dev shell or GHC; the plain dev shell prints an error.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Run targeted suites, then make format, make lint, make build-test, and make test-host in the normal 16-lane AMX-off configuration.
GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:675-683 (lint-notes target; 'ERROR: lint-notes requires the compiled checker or GHC and Cabal. Preferred: enter nix develop .#lint'), :693-731 (lint recipe: diff --check, cassert grep, clang-format, cargo fmt, lint-ci-pins, lint-agent-guidance; no lint-notes). flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1571 (lintRuntimeDeps = lintToolchainDeps ++ [ lintNotes ]) and the lint check body, whose last commands are bin/ci/check-lock-freshness.sh then 'make lint-notes'. bin/ci/run-lint-checks.sh@2880c3aa9b:7 (default check_ref .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain). .github/workflows/gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:43-56 (Lint job runs run-lint-checks.sh). bin/lint-notes@2880c3aa9b:15-31 (definition = 'Note [Name]' plus '~~~' line; reference = 'See Note [Name]'). git grep 'Zero-Initialized V Slots' at 2880c3aa9b over h src t: 9 lines, 1 definition (kv_cache.hpp:2055) and 8 references.
Correction from verification
Three corrections and one added mitigation. (1) Drop the sentence "The checker also needs the .#lint dev shell or GHC; the plain dev shell prints an error." The plain nix develop shell inherits the ingest Haskell shell (flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:544 inputsFrom = [ targetIngestFlake.devShells.default ], cabal tool at :329), and README.md@2880c3aa9b:237-247 documents nix develop as providing the pinned GHC and Cabal for make lint-notes. In that shell the recipe takes the bin/lint-notes (Cabal script) branch (GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:678-679); the error text (:681-683) prints only when neither tron-lint-notes nor GHC+Cabal is on PATH, i.e. outside both shells. The .#lint shell (flake.nix:1618-1625, packages = lintRuntimeDeps ++ [ lefthookDev ]) adds the precompiled tron-lint-notes. Reword the recommendation's parenthetical to: "(the .#lint shell supplies the precompiled checker; the default shell runs the bin/lint-notes script through its GHC and Cabal)". (2) Fix the citation "GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:675-683" to ":675-685" (the recipe's fi is at 685). (3) Replace "cross-references from four other files" with "cross-references at four sites in three files (amx_attn_iface.hpp, self_attention.hpp, model.hpp twice)" per design-r3.txt:363. Added mitigation, to narrow "passes make lint locally and fails the required check": lefthook.yml@2880c3aa9b:214-238 defines a pre-commit lint-notes command with globs h//*.{hpp,h,i}, src//*.cpp, t/**/*.cpp that runs make lint-notes, and the default dev shell registers that hook when CI is unset (flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1611-1616 installNixHooks, appended to the default shell hook at :1851). So a commit made from the dev shell with hooks active does run lint-notes; the gap is the design's explicit checklist, commits made with hooks bypassed, and the hook's silent "Skipping: lint-notes checker or GHC/Cabal not found" path outside Nix (lefthook.yml:235). Also make the second recommendation conditional: the design does not require the relocated v_vnni.hpp documentation to be a Note, so say "If the relocated documentation is written as a Note, give the heading the ~~~ underline (bin/lint-notes@2880c3aa9b:132-139) and write each cross-reference as See Note [Name]; keep the name Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots] unchanged, since eight comments reference it." Mechanism nit for the finding text: a heading without ~~~ is not parsed as a definition, so the checker reports the eight references as "Reference to undefined" (bin/lint-notes:283-284) rather than as a malformed definition (:115-116 covers only a missing ] or a name under 5 characters); the check fails either way.
Narrow two parts and keep the rest. (1) Drop the sentence "The checker also needs the .#lint dev shell or GHC; the plain dev shell prints an error." The default shell built by mkTronDevShell includes the ingest shell and sets GHC="ghc" and CABAL_INSTALL="cabal" [flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1742-1743, 1868-1870; ghc-on-PATH comment at 633]. With ghc and cabal present, make lint-notes takes the bin/lint-notes fallback branch instead of printing the error [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:678-679]; the error text prints only when neither the compiled checker nor GHC+Cabal is found [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:680-684]. Replace with: "In the default shell, make lint-notes compiles bin/lint-notes with the ingest GHC and Cabal; nix develop .#lint supplies the prebuilt tron-lint-notes [flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1521-1533, 1571, 1617-1620]." (2) Do not ask the design to mandate that the relocated v_vnni.hpp documentation be written as a repository Note; no repository rule requires that form, and a plain comment pointing at the header passes lint-notes. Replace the second recommendation with: "After design-r3.txt:363 add: If the relocated documentation becomes a Note, underline its heading with ~~~ and write each cross-reference as See Note [Name]; keep the name Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots] unchanged, since eight comments reference it [bin/lint-notes@2880c3aa9b:15-31; git grep at 2880c3aa9b]." (3) Keep the first recommendation for design-r3.txt:477 as written, and add that the design's list mirrors README.ci.md's local-reproduction block [README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:655-664], so the design should also name the CI-equivalent command nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain -L --no-update-lock-file [README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:169-175] as the authoritative local reproduction of the Lint job. (4) Optionally note the mitigation: the default dev shell installs lefthook hooks [flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1851] and the lint-notes hook runs on commits touching h//*.hpp, src//*.cpp and t/**/*.cpp [lefthook.yml@2880c3aa9b:214-238]; this does not remove the omission because the design asks implementers to report skipped checks by name from its own list [design-r3.txt:477] and never mentions the hook.
Narrow and fix four points. (1) "cross-references from four other files" should read "three files at four sites": amx_attn_iface.hpp:224-227, self_attention.hpp:31-35, model.hpp:209-213 and model.hpp:2816-2820 [design-r3.txt:363]. (2) Drop "the plain dev shell prints an error". The default dev shell inherits the ingest Haskell shell [flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1708 inputsFrom targetIngestShell; :1742-1743 GHC = "ghc", CABAL_INSTALL = "cabal"], so make lint-notes takes the GHC-and-Cabal branch and runs the source checker bin/lint-notes [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:678-679]; the compiled tron-lint-notes is on PATH only in the .#lint shell [flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1571, 1617-1622] and inside the lefthook wrapper [flake.nix@2880c3aa9b:1598-1599]. Reword to: "the plain dev shell runs the slower source checker through Cabal; nix develop .#lint --accept-flake-config supplies the compiled checker; the exact CI reproduction is nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain -L --no-update-lock-file [README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:171-175; lefthook.yml@2880c3aa9b:6-7]". (3) Drop "renamed Note": the design keeps the name [design-r3.txt:362]. State the risk as a malformed definition (the ~~~ rule must start at the "Note [" column on the next line [bin/lint-notes@2880c3aa9b:132-139]) or a dangling "See Note [...]" introduced by the relocation at design-r3.txt:363 or by the child port of Note [K VNNI storage] and Note [Shared block save]. (4) Add context: README.ci.md's own "Reproducing CI Locally" block lists make lint without lint-notes [README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:653-664], so the design followed the documented list; cite README.ci.md:169-175 when adding the command. Mention the lefthook pre-commit hook [lefthook.yml@2880c3aa9b:214-238] as a partial safeguard that skips silently when the checker is absent [:229-236]. Also: the lint-notes target spans GNUmakefile:675-685, not 675-683. Make the second recommended sentence conditional: "If the relocated documentation is written as a repository Note (a Note [Name] heading underlined with ~~~ on the next line), write each cross-reference as See Note [Name]; keep the name Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots], which eight comments reference." The first recommended sentence for design-r3.txt:477 stands.
Recommendation
At design-r3.txt:477 replace the sentence with: 'Run targeted suites, then make format, make lint, make lint-notes (inside nix develop .#lint --accept-flake-config, which supplies the checker), make build-test, and make test-host in the normal 16-lane AMX-off configuration. The required Lint job runs nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain, which includes lint-notes; make lint alone does not.' After design-r3.txt:363 add: 'Write the relocated documentation as a repository Note (a Note [Name] heading underlined with ~~~) and each cross-reference as See Note [Name]. Keep the name Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots]; eight comments reference it.'
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3. Correction: drop the sentence that the plain dev shell prints an error; the default shell inherits the ingest GHC and Cabal and runs the slower source checker, while nix develop .#lint supplies the compiled one. The parent edits Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots], which eight comments reference, and relocates layout documentation with cross-references, so a malformed Note passes make lint locally and fails the required Lint check.

Note G14. 8-lane class-completeness rule serves only dead fake code; moving the fakes under TRON_AMX_DISPATCH removes it

Where
5. Build and include boundaries (design-r3.txt:281); 7. step 1 (:372); child paragraph (:414); 8. build table 8-lane row (:454)
Earlier ids
touches N01 N61
Claim
The rule that the packed V class definitions (and later the K definitions) must stay complete in the 8-lane build has exactly one client: the fake kernel definitions in t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp. Those fakes are dead code whenever TRON_AMX_DISPATCH is off, and TRON_AMX_DISPATCH cannot be on at 8 lanes. The parent edits that test file anyway (step 6). Moving the fake block under the file's existing #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH removes the width-neutral class rule, the chunk_size-neutral constraint wording, the parallel child rule, and the t_amx_dispatch_dtype comparison in the 8-lane row. The design does not weigh this smaller option.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Keep packed V class definitions and typed kernel declarations available at both chunk widths. The unconditional fake AMX definitions require complete by-value parameter types. This does not require 8-lane implementations of packed-V SIMD operations.
t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:166-190 (namespace tron::amx_attn_h128g4, qk_rowmajor_128x4 at :180, weights_times_v_128x4 at :185) and the anonymous-namespace probe at :47-164; the file's own guard #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH starts only at :192. t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:596 (test registered unconditionally). With TRON_AMX_DISPATCH off nothing references amx_attn_h128g4: h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:259-267, :281-290, :1429-1464 (all call sites inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH); src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:219-223 (amx_attn.cpp compiled only under if(TRON_AMX_DISPATCH)). self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:30-36 (#error at 8 lanes). t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:22-266 (already wrapped in #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH). A function declaration may name an incomplete class type as a by-value parameter, so the typed declarations in amx_attn_iface.hpp compile at 8 lanes with forward declarations alone. design-r3.txt:377 (step 6 edits t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp). Round-2 N01 and N61 established that only class completeness is needed at 8 lanes; neither considered removing that need.

Four narrowings, none changing the verdict. 1. Fairness to the design: the design followed the reviewers' own instruction. Citation completeness: the finding lists self_attention.hpp:259-267, :281-290, :1429-1464 as "all call sites", but those blocks hold only the packed-Q slot, the scratch vector, and the region bracket. The kernel calls themselves are in :1571-1701 (pack :1586, qk :1654, pv :1680); further guarded blocks are :1531-1538 and :1748-1771. Add them; the conclusion (every use is guarded) is unchanged. Also cite src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:219-224 (the endif is at :224). 3. That is not a functional loss, because in that configuration the fakes are dead code and a signature mismatch is only observable with AMX on (it would make the linker pull the real amx_attn.o). Note G27. Debug row: 'make BUILD_TYPE=Debug <test>' does not reconfigure an existing gen/, and 'Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo' holds for one path only

Where
8. Build configurations table, row 'NaN-preserving Debug check, 16 lanes' (design-r3.txt:455; worktree rule :449)
Earlier ids
touches N39 N60 N02
Claim
The row tells the agent to run targeted conversion cases with make BUILD_TYPE=Debug and the test target. That command changes the build type only when Make re-runs the CMake configure step, and Make re-runs it only when the gen/config/test stamp is missing or one of its listed inputs changed; none of those inputs tracks BUILD_TYPE. So in a gen/ already configured as RelWithDebInfo, make BUILD_TYPE=Debug t_llama_unit keeps the cached RelWithDebInfo and TRON_IGNORE_NAN stays defined; the agent would test the macro-defined policy while believing it tested the Debug policy. Conversely, in a worktree configured by hand with the row's cmake line, the first plain make target re-runs cmake with Make's default -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo and silently undoes the Debug configuration, while in a Make-configured Debug worktree a later plain make keeps Debug. The sentence 'Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo' is true for the first path and false for the second, and the row does not say which. The t_% Make target also builds and then runs the whole binary with no Catch2 filter. The design's separate-worktree rule (:449) and 'Confirm the macro is absent' limit the damage, so the risk is low.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Run targeted conversion cases using make BUILD_TYPE=Debug with the applicable test target, or build with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit after Debug configuration. Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo. Confirm the macro is absent.
GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:80 (BUILD_TYPE := RelWithDebInfo), :538-543 (gen/config/test depends on $(CMAKELISTS), config/models.yaml, t/proxy/ctest-trampoline.sh, the FUSE and preset stamps and the plugin-bundle stamps, then runs cmake --preset $(CMAKE_PRESET) -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=$(BUILD_TYPE) ...), :299-303 (preset stamp tracks CMAKE_PRESET only), :289-290 (FUSE stamp tracks ENABLE_FUSE_STATS only), :880-888 (gen/t_%: gen/config/test $(HEADERS) $(SOURCES) runs cmake --build gen --target; t_%: gen/t_% runs gen/$@ with no arguments). CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:34-38 (native preset fixes binaryDir gen and CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE RelWithDebInfo). design-r3.txt:449, :455, :437, :513 (section 9 N02 N12 row).
Correction from verification
Narrow "an existing gen/" in the title and claim to "a gen/ that a Make target already configured (gen/config/test present and up to date)". A gen/ configured only by the row's hand cmake line has no stamp, so make BUILD_TYPE=Debug t_llama_unit there DOES re-run cmake with Debug [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:538-543]. On the design's prescribed path (fresh worktree per configuration, design-r3.txt:449) the row's make BUILD_TYPE=Debug instruction is therefore correct; the defect is (1) the unqualified sentence "Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo" [design-r3.txt:455], which is true only after a hand configure and false after a Make configure, and (2) the unstated hazard of reusing a Make-configured RelWithDebInfo gen/. Replace "silently undoes" with "re-runs cmake with RelWithDebInfo; the only sign is the ordinary configure output" (the recipe prints cmake's stdout; no warning names the change). In the recommendation, replace "absent from compile_commands.json for the binary you run" with "absent from the compile lines of the test's translation units in gen/compile_commands.json (CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS is on in the base-ninja preset [CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:22])", and extend "a CMakeLists.txt, models.yaml or preset input changed" to also name t/proxy/ctest-trampoline.sh, ENABLE_FUSE_STATS and the plugin-bundle stamps [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:538]. Suggested one-sentence design fix for the row: "Make re-runs cmake only when gen/config/test is missing or one of its listed inputs changed; it does not track BUILD_TYPE. So a hand-configured Debug worktree loses Debug on the first plain make target, and a Make-configured worktree keeps whatever build type its first make used. Do not reuse a gen/ from another row."
Narrow G27 to a note with two wording changes in the Debug row [design-r3.txt:455]. (1) Replace "Run targeted conversion cases using make BUILD_TYPE=Debug with the applicable test target, or build with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit after Debug configuration." with "In this row's fresh worktree, build with make BUILD_TYPE=Debug gen/t_llama_unit or with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit after the cmake line. Then run gen/t_llama_unit with the conversion case names as arguments. The make t_<name> target runs the whole binary with no Catch2 filter (GNUmakefile:886-887 at 2880c3aa9b)." (2) Replace "Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo. Confirm the macro is absent." with "A Make target reconfigures gen/ with -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo whenever gen/config/test is missing or a CMakeLists.txt, config/models.yaml or preset input changed (GNUmakefile:538-543 at 2880c3aa9b). Before running, confirm CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug in gen/CMakeCache.txt and no -DTRON_IGNORE_NAN in gen/compile_commands.json." Drop the finding's claim that the row "does not say which" path restores RelWithDebInfo: the design's "can" [design-r3.txt:455] plus "may reconfigure it. Confirm the cached feature values after invoking them" [design-r3.txt:449] already cover both paths, and the finding's sentence "In a Make-configured Debug worktree a later plain make keeps Debug" holds only while no configure input changes. Drop the "existing gen/" scenario as a defect: design-r3.txt:449 requires a separate worktree with its own gen/ for every configuration pair, and the Debug row is one of those configurations.
Narrow the title to: "Debug row: make BUILD_TYPE=Debug does not reconfigure a gen/ that Make already configured (the configure stamp does not track BUILD_TYPE), and make t_<name> runs the whole binary with no case filter". Severity note; no change to the disposition of N02, N12 or N60.
Recommendation
Replace the row text with: 'Configure a separate worktree with this row's cmake line. Build with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit and run gen/t_llama_unit with a Catch2 name filter for the conversion cases. Do not switch an existing gen/ with make BUILD_TYPE=Debug: the Make configure recipe (gen/config/test) runs only when its stamp is missing or a CMakeLists.txt, models.yaml or preset input changed, and it does not track BUILD_TYPE. In a hand-configured Debug worktree the first plain make target (make build-test, make test-host, make t_llama_unit) re-runs cmake with Make's default -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo. In a Make-configured Debug worktree a later plain make keeps Debug. Confirm TRON_IGNORE_NAN is absent from compile_commands.json for the binary you run.'

Holds 2 of 3; lowered to note by the lead. The design's prescribed path (a fresh worktree per configuration, :449) makes make BUILD_TYPE=Debug correct, because a hand-configured gen/ has no Make stamp. Note G38. Packed accessor names follow different patterns: page::v_view versus page::k_packed

5. Existing callers table, page::v_data row (design-r3.txt:271); 7. Child interface table, page::k row (:404)
Claim
The parent's packed V accessor is named page::v_view and the child's packed K accessor is named page::k_packed. Both return the read-only packed view of the same shape family. The names follow different patterns ('_view' versus '_packed'), so a reader of section 7 cannot infer the K name from section 5, and the child's name differs in style from the existing native pair page::k / kv_block::k_view.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Add the const method page::v_view<geometry>(slot, head) in the 16-lane build only. [...] Replace the raw packed-plane accessor with page::k_packed<geometry>(slot, head) , constrained to packed storage.
design-r3.txt:271 (page::v_view returns v_vnni_view<const bf16,page_size,geometry.head_size>), :404 (page::k_packed returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,page_size,geometry.head_size>), :267 (existing page::k and kv_block::k_view keep their names). kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1689 (the child's raw accessor is k_vnni_plane).
Correction from verification
Narrow and re-anchor the finding as follows. (1) The name page::v_view is not codex's own choice. Round-2 findings N18 and N23 both recommended it: N18 said "Rename to page::v_view<geometry>(slot, kv_head) (or v_plane)" [r2-findings.md:327] and N23 said "Add page::v_view<geometry>(slot, kv_head) const under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 only, returning v_vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, geometry.head_size>" [r2-findings.md:358]. The design's section 9 records the adoption: "Return a read-only packed v_view only at 16 lanes" [design-r3.txt:520]. A recommendation to rename v_view to v_packed therefore reverses the round-2 name recommendation, and the finding must say so. (2) The name page::k_packed is new in this revision (round-2 text only said "page::k_vnni_plane Remove the raw-pointer accessor. Its replacement returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128>" [evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:377]; the word diff shows the NEW segment "with page::k_packed(slot, head)" [design-wdiff.txt:1384]). It matches the reviewer's sketch exactly: Ben's comment shows auto k_packed(kv_slot_id slot, size_t head) const noexcept { return kv_block<geometry>(slot, head).k.as_view(); } and the usage example qk_vnni_128x4(page.v_packed(...), ...); // Compile error [evidence/github-review.json, Ben's sketch comment: k_packed 2 hits, v_packed 1 hit, v_view 0 hits, k_view 0 hits]. So the reviewer's own vocabulary uses "_packed" for both accessors. This is the strongest reason to prefer v_packed / k_packed, and the finding should cite it instead of style alone. (3) Replace "cannot infer the K name from section 5" with "the two accessors use different suffixes for the same kind of return value, and the design gives no reason". Both names are stated explicitly [design-r3.txt:271, :404], so no inference is needed. (4) Replace "update lines 271, 404 and section 8" with "update lines 271, 404 and the section 9 disposition row at line 520". Section 8 [design-r3.txt:429-504] does not mention v_view or k_packed; the only other mention is the N16-N24 row [design-r3.txt:520]. (5) Optional supporting point: the parent merges before the child [design-r3.txt:124 "Add the tensor interface to main first"], so a v_view name frozen in main would need a second rename PR if the pair is later made consistent. Decide the shared suffix before the parent PR is opened. Keep the "page::k_vnni_view would clash with the type name" remark as a reason to avoid that option (a member named k_vnni_view inside page would hide the namespace-scope class template of the same name within page's scope); it is not central. The child accessor path in the evidence should read h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1689, not kv_cache.hpp alone.
Keep the finding with four changes. (1) Add the origin of each name: page::v_view follows the round-2 review's own recommendation N18/N23 [r2-findings.md:327, :358], which reversed round-1 F39's page::v_tensor; page::k_packed follows the reviewer's sketch, comment 5765866077, which names both accessors k_packed and v_packed [evidence/github-review.json]. The finding should say the review is reversing its own round-2 name if it now asks for v_packed. (2) Replace "section 8" in the recommendation with "section 9 row N16 N17 N18 N23 N24 [design-r3.txt:520]". Section 8 spans design-r3.txt:429-504 and does not name the accessor. (3) Offer the design two ways to close it: rename to page::v_packed so both accessors match the sketch, or add one sentence at design-r3.txt:404 stating why K does not use a "_view" name (kv_block::k_view is the native-only accessor per design-r3.txt:401, so a packed page::k_view would be confusing). (4) Weaken the "existing native pair" argument. Both new accessors are page members. The page-level precedent is the bare name page::k [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1549-1595]; k_view is a kv_block member [:2208, :2215]. Neither new name follows the page-level pattern, so the argument should rest on the V/K mismatch and the sketch, not on the native pair.
Narrow and fix the finding as follows. (1) Add that both names follow round-2 recommendations: N18 asked for page::v_view, N03 offered "the sketch's k_packed" [r2-findings.md, N18 and N03 RECOMMENDATION]; the inconsistency is a review artifact, so the design is not at fault for following instructions. (2) Add the evidence that Ben's sketch uses both k_packed and v_packed [github-review.json, comments[3] body: "auto k_packed(kv_slot_id slot, size_t head) const noexcept" and "qk_vnni_128x4(page.v_packed(...), ...); // Compile error"]; this makes v_packed the name most likely to match the reviewer's expectation. (3) Drop "update ... section 8 accordingly": section 8 [design-r3.txt:429-504] mentions neither accessor. The lines to update are design-r3.txt:271 (section 5 row), :404 (section 7 row), and the section-9 disposition row at :520 ("Return a read-only packed v_view only at 16 lanes"). (4) Reword "page::k_vnni_view would clash with the type name": a member function named k_vnni_view would hide the namespace-scope type name inside page, which is an inconvenience rather than a compile clash; the point still favors k_packed. (5) Note that this contradicts the earlier N18 recommendation, so the review page should say the new preference (v_packed) supersedes N18's v_view.
Recommendation
Use one pattern for both families. page::k_vnni_view would clash with the type name, so prefer page::v_packed<geometry>(slot, head) and page::k_packed<geometry>(slot, head); update lines 271, 404 and section 8 accordingly.
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3 at note. Both names follow review advice: page::v_view was round-2 N18/N23's recommendation, page::k_packed follows Ben's sketch, which uses both k_packed and v_packed. If the design wants one pattern, v_packed matches the reviewer's expectation; the lines to change are :271, :404 and :520.

Note G40. Row const-conversion left ambiguous by the new 'constructed only by the matrix view' rule

Where
5. Interface design, View copy and conversion row (design-r3.txt:248); 8. Typed function boundaries (:438)
Earlier ids
touches N20
Claim
The same table row says 'Allow writable-to-read-only conversion only' and then 'Rows are constructed only by the matrix view'. Section 8 asks tests to 'Accept writable-to-read-only conversion' without saying for which types. A converting constructor v_vnni_row<const bf16,D>(v_vnni_row<bf16,D>) is a constructor the matrix view does not call, so an implementer cannot tell whether rows convert (which needs a friend declaration across the T parameter, as the reviewer's sketch does for views) or do not convert (then a test that converts a row fails to compile). No named consumer needs the conversion: read-only rows come from a const matrix (:245).
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
| View copy and conversion | Copying a view rebinds access. Allow writable-to-read-only conversion only. Owners, views, and rows expose no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, or pointer conversion. Rows are constructed only by the matrix view. |
design-r3.txt:248, :240, :245, :249, :438, :518 (section 9 'Restrict row construction'). Reviewer sketch (comment 5765866077): template <typename, typename> friend class vnni_view; for the view conversion.
Correction from verification
Narrow and sharpen in four places. (1) State that the literal reading of design-r3.txt:248 already leans one way: a converting constructor is not construction "by the matrix view", so rows do not convert under that reading; the ambiguity is between that literal reading, the untyped test instruction at design-r3.txt:438, and the N20 corrected recommendation that asked for the row friend. (2) Add the disposition fact: round-2 N20's verifier corrections (design-text lens and intent lens) both asked for template <typename, size_t> friend class v_vnni_row; so that v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> accepts v_vnni_row<bf16,D>; the design's section-9 row for N20 (design-r3.txt:518, "Restrict row construction") records no decision on that item, so grade N20 as partly, not resolved. (3) Cite Ben's sketch precisely: comment 5765866077 body lines 73-77 (the requires(std::is_const_v<T> && std::same_as<U, bf16>) converting constructor) and lines 90-91 (template <typename, typename> / friend class vnni_view;), which the finding quotes as one line. (4) Add design-r3.txt:401 ("Apply the V owner's lvalue-only as_view(), const conversion, and private packed-pointer rules to K too") as a second site that inherits the same ambiguity for the child's K row; one clause at :248 settles both. Recommended wording for :248, preferred option given no consumer: "Rows have no cross-constness conversion; obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view. Writable-to-read-only conversion applies to views." Then at :438 replace "Accept writable-to-read-only conversion" with "Accept writable-to-read-only view conversion; reject row-to-row conversion" (or "accept" it, if the friend option is chosen), and add the chosen option to the N20 row at :518.
Keep the claim and recommendation. Three narrowings. (1) Attribute the gap to N20: this is the unaddressed part (1) of N20's corrected recommendation (batches/B09.md:19-20, "give the row a template <typename, size_t> friend class v_vnni_row; declaration" or otherwise make the allowed conversion compile with a private constructor). The disposition checker should grade N20 "partly", not "resolved", unless the design adds either clause; the private-constructor and view-as-only-producer parts of N20 are resolved by design-r3.txt:248. (2) State that the view side is not ambiguous: design-r3.txt:73 retains the sketch's "mutable-to-read-only conversion", and the sketch supplies the mechanism for views (comment 5765866077 body lines 73-75, 90-91); only rows are open, because the sketch has no row type. (3) Replace "then a test that converts a row fails to compile" with "then a STATIC_REQUIRE that asserts row convertibility fails, or a conversion expression in the test does not compile" to match the STATIC_REQUIRE / STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE form design-r3.txt:438 prescribes. Preferred fix text for design-r3.txt:248, given that no consumer needs it: "Rows have no cross-constness conversion. Obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view (a const matrix or a v_vnni_view<const bf16,R,D>)." Add to design-r3.txt:438: "The accepted and rejected const conversions are between views; rows are checked only for const-row mutation."
Narrow the claim: the row heading "View copy and conversion" and the sentence "Rows are constructed only by the matrix view" (design-r3.txt:248) already make "rows have no cross-constness conversion" the more likely reading, so the design is not silent, only implicit. The residual defects are (a) design-r3.txt:438 does not say the accepted and rejected const conversions are between views, and (b) the section-9 row for N20 (design-r3.txt:518) neither adopts nor declines the round-2 verifier clause that asked for a row-to-row friend declaration (r2-findings.md, N20 verifier corrections). Recommended fix, one clause each: in :248 add "Rows have no cross-constness conversion; obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view (see :245)", which is the simpler choice and consistent with "constructed only by the matrix view" and with Ben's sketch, which has no row type; in :438 change "Accept writable-to-read-only conversion" to "Accept writable-to-read-only conversion between views" and add "reject a row constructed from a raw pointer" to the reject list, as the corrected N20 recommendation asked. Keep the finding's link to N20 as "partly": row construction is restricted, the conversion clause is unaddressed.
Recommendation
Add one clause to design-r3.txt:248, either 'Rows have no cross-constness conversion; obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view' or 'v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> is constructible from v_vnni_row<bf16,D> through a friend declaration', and say in :438 that the accepted conversion is between views (and rows, if chosen).
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3 at note. This is the unaddressed part of N20's corrected recommendation, so N20 is graded partly. The heading "View copy and conversion" and "Rows are constructed only by the matrix view" make no-row-conversion the likelier reading; the design should say so in one clause and make :438 name views.

Note G42. Uniform-array rule: the 'existing uniform accessor' justification holds only for uniform reclaimability

Where
6. Arena construction without clearing, uniform bullet (design-r3.txt:349)
Earlier ids
touches N25
Claim
The uniform bullet justifies the one-array rule by 'the existing uniform accessor's indexing across slots'. That indexing (one kv_block_t array starting at kv_blocks_alias and spanning all slots) exists only when layout_t::uniform_reclaimability holds. A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has two arenas, and its accessor addresses each slot through slot_base; t_llama_unit has a case for that layout. The rule itself is still right for it, because each arena is a contiguous run of the same kv_block_t. The design should say the rule applies to each arena and to every restore, and should not point the implementer at kv_blocks_alias, which is one slot's base (the storage-view slot) and is refreshed only after allocation.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
For uniform geometry, construct one array over the just-allocated arena's complete contiguous block region. Its bound is the checked arena byte count divided by sizeof(kv_block_t). This matches the existing uniform accessor's indexing across slots.
kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289 (uniform_reclaimability branch: blocks = kv_blocks_alias; blocks[(slot.i * n_kv_heads + kv_head) * n_pages + pg]); :1290-1299 (else branch: slot_base(kv_storage_offset(physical_slot)); blocks[kv_head * n_pages + pg]); :682-683 (uniform_reclaimability); :1222-1228 (refresh_kv_blocks_alias sets kv_blocks_alias = slot_base(storage_view_offset_), called after allocate_kv_group at :1114 and :971). t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:603 TEST_CASE 'uniform-geometry mixed retention addresses both arenas'. evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:331 (sentence unchanged from round 2); round-2 N25 verifier recorded the two-branch nuance as an evidence precision only.
Correction from verification
Three details need narrowing. (1) The design text does not name kv_blocks_alias anywhere [grep of design-r3.txt: no hit]. The risk is indirect: an implementer who follows "the existing uniform accessor" to kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289 finds kv_blocks_alias as the base pointer. Reword "should not point the implementer at kv_blocks_alias" to "should not send the implementer to an accessor branch whose base pointer is kv_blocks_alias". (2) "refreshed only after allocation" is too narrow. refresh_kv_blocks_alias runs after allocate_kv_group returns in the constructor [1114] and in restore [971], and also on set_storage_view [897], reset_storage_view [902] and free_reclaimable_kv_storage [953]. The relevant fact is that inside allocate_kv_group [1199-1208], where design-r3.txt:348 places the construction helper, the alias is still nullptr (constructor, member default at 1128) or stale (restore, after the free at 953 refreshed it against a missing arena). (3) "Apply this to each arena separately" is already implied by "the just-allocated arena's" [design-r3.txt:349] plus the helper's placement in allocate_kv_group, which runs once per arena [1101, 1102, 968]. The recommendation should say it makes the implied scope explicit. Suggested replacement for the third sentence: "The existing uniform accessor indexes one array across slots only when layout_t::uniform_reclaimability holds (kv_cache.hpp:1284-1289); with mixed retention it addresses each slot through slot_base (1290-1299). The one-array rule still holds for each arena, because every slot of a uniform-geometry layout has the same kv_block_t and the slots of one arena are packed contiguously (650-671, 1188-1196). Use the arena pointer allocate_kv_group has just installed (retained_data or reclaimable_data), not kv_blocks_alias, which is one slot's base and is refreshed after allocate_kv_group returns (1114, 971, 1222-1228). A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has both arenas (t/t_llama_unit.cpp:602-603)." Also add the uniform-geometry mixed-retention case to the section 8 storage row, which names "mixed geometry" and "release/restore" but not that case [design-r3.txt:441].
Narrow the claim to the justification sentence only and drop the two recommendation parts the design already covers. Suggested wording: "The third sentence of the uniform bullet [design-r3.txt:349] describes only the uniform_reclaimability branch of kv_block() [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289]. A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has two arenas, and its accessor addresses each slot through slot_base [:1290-1299; t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:602-622]. The prescription is right for both: construction runs per allocate_kv_group call, so each arena gets its own array in the constructor and in every restore [design-r3.txt:348-349; kv_cache.hpp:1101-1102, :968], and slot_base resolves to a block index inside that arena's array [:1188-1197, :659-661]." Optional text change, one clause appended to design-r3.txt:349: "Under uniform geometry with mixed retention, each arena holds a subset of the slots; the accessor reaches them through slot_base, which resolves to an index inside the same per-arena array (kv_cache.hpp:1290-1299)." Narrow the claim to the justification sentence only. Note G44. K whole-plane copy sentence: the caller is not named in the body and the mechanism differs from the V owner-copy rule

Where
7. Child interface table, Copy and score readers row (design-r3.txt:407); compare 5. One cache row-write rule (:261); 8. Child only row (:443); 9. row N03.. (:514)
Earlier ids
touches N17 N03
Claim
For V the design says a defaulted same-type owner copy is sufficient when a whole owner must be copied and forbids a copy_plane operation. For K it requires 'a typed K plane-copy operation' for 'the child's existing whole-plane copy caller' without naming that caller. The caller is the whole-page memcpy of kv_block::k inside copy_storage_slot in the child's kv_cache.hpp; both operands are lvalue kv_block members. Both owners are trivially copyable by the design's own rules, so dst_block.k = src_block.k (the defaulted copy assignment) is the direct replacement and matches the V rule. Two different mechanisms for the same need are not an error, but the design does not say why K needs the extra operation, and section 9 says the design names the 'actual K plane-copy caller' while the body does not.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Use typed K token copies and a typed K plane-copy operation for the child's existing whole-plane copy caller.
kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2100-2107 (comment 'So a whole-page copy is one memcpy. Every other range moves token by token.'; inside copy_storage_slot: if (src_begin == 0 && dst_begin == 0 && count == page_size) { memcpy(dst_block.k, src_block.k, sizeof(dst_block.k)); }). facts-r3.md:79 names the same site. design-r3.txt:238-239 (owner: trivial construction, copying, destruction; same-type copy and move defaulted), :261, :401 ('Apply the V owner's ... rules to K too'), :407, :443 ('typed whole-plane copy'), :514 ('specify row/block argument families and actual K plane-copy caller'); grep 'copy_storage_slot' in design-r3.txt hits only :272 (8-lane V range copies).
Correction from verification
Narrow two parts. (a) "Without naming that caller" is true of the body text only. Section 10's S18 entry carries the link "[Whole-plane and row K copies]" pinned to h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb#L2099-L2115 [design-r3.txt:582; design-r3-links.tsv:219], which is exactly the copy_storage_slot whole-page memcpy (2107) and the token loop (2110-2115). So the section-9 claim "specify ... actual K plane-copy caller" [design-r3.txt:514] is backed by a pinned source link, not by a function name in the body. Reword to: "The body row [:407] does not name copy_storage_slot; the caller is identified only through the S18 link [:582]. Add the function name to the row." (b) The kind is an unexplained asymmetry, not a contradiction. The prohibitions on a plane-copy operation are V-specific ("No ... V plane-copy helper" [design-r3.txt:288]; "Add no V plane-copy operation" [:372]; "Do not add copy_plane for V" [:261]). No design rule forbids a K plane-copy operation, so the K sentence violates nothing; it adds an operation the design's own owner rules make redundant, and gives no reason. (c) In the recommendation, say the owner-copy replacement applies in the packed branch only: the memcpy at 2107 is inside if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<geometry.head_size>) [kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2099], and the native branch [2118-2126] keeps its row-range memcpy over k_at().data, which the design already constrains to the array member [design-r3.txt:401 "Constrain native k_view() and k_at() to the array branch"]. Recommended sentence for :407: "Use typed K token copies. For the child's one whole-page K copy in copy_storage_slot (the memcpy at kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2107, reached only in the packed branch when the copy covers a full page), use the defaulted owner copy dst_block.k = src_block.k, as section 5 allows for V; add no named K plane-copy operation." Change :443 "typed whole-plane copy" to "whole-plane owner copy". If codex keeps the named operation instead, the row must state the reason it is needed.
Narrow three parts of the finding. (a) Replace "without naming that caller" with: "the body row [design-r3.txt:407] does not name the function or cite a source; the caller is identified only by the S18 link '[Whole-plane and row K copies]' (kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb#L2099-L2115) [design-r3.txt:582; design-r3-links.tsv:219], cited as [S18] in the paragraph above the table [:401]. Note G45. Query-layout selection placed 'in kernels'; the child keeps it in self_attention.hpp

Where

Claim
The row says to retain the query-layout selection 'in kernels'. In the child, the selection between pack_q_rows_128x4 and the row-major packing, and between qk_vnni_128x4 and qk_rowmajor_128x4, is an if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) in self_attention.hpp, which the design's own S10 calls attention code. The round-2 text said 'outside the tensor', which was correct. 'In kernels' invites moving that compile-time selection into src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp, a change the design elsewhere forbids ('Keep dispatch').
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Retain score arithmetic and query-layout selection in kernels.
h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1365-1366 (if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<geometry.kv.head_size>) { amx_attn_h128g4::pack_q_rows_128x4(group, slot);), :1436-1444 (if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<operation_head_size>) { qk_vnni_128x4(...) } else { qk_rowmajor_128x4(...) }), :1574 (constexpr bool k_vnni_on = ...). design-r3.txt:574 (S10: 'Attention code chooses the query orientation and score implementation.'), :375 ('Keep dispatch, algorithms, query packing, and output contracts.'). evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:380 ('Keep optimized arithmetic and query-layout selection outside the tensor.').

Note G46. Deduction trap for typed K row arguments not restated for the child's callers

Where
7. Child interface table, page::set_k_row / page::get_k_row row (design-r3.txt:405)
Claim
The K row parameters are given as const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> with Aligned and Dma as template parameters. Template argument deduction does not consider conversions, so a writable view or a raw pointer at the call site does not bind. Every child caller passes one of those: store_k_block passes the slice view's raw .data or a stack array, the FPGA callback passes a raw scratch pointer, and the layout test passes row.data(). Section 5 states the explicit-construction rule for the V rows only (:257). The K row does not repeat it.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Use const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> for save and view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> for load. The boolean template arguments preserve the caller's alignment and DMA properties.
design-r3.txt:257 ('Explicitly construct read-only source views where template deduction would otherwise see writable views.', V rows only). h/tron/models/model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2864 (row_of returns slice<head_size>()(ks[ix].as_view(), ...), a writable view), :2920 (set_k_row<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j, kout.data)), :2924 (set_k_row<...>(..., conv[0])); h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2655 (get_k_row<hw_kv>(slot, kv_head, off, dest) with bf16* dest); t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:401 (page->set_k_row(SLOT_0, 0, tok, row.data())).
Correction from verification
Narrow and broaden as follows. (1) The design already says "Wrap scratch without copying" [design-r3.txt:405], so the raw-pointer scratch callers (full.hpp dest, conv[0], the test arrays) are covered in substance. The uncovered case is the writable slice view kout [model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2864, :2920], which an implementer would pass directly and which fails deduction; the missing element for all callers is the reason (deduction ignores converting constructors). (2) Broaden the caller list: t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@30c4ac82cb:101, about 25 sites in t/t_llama_unit.cpp@30c4ac82cb (for example :362-366, :579-607, :2247-2285), and the uniform-geometry forwarding overloads kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1752-1764, whose const bf16* / bf16* parameters must also become the typed views. (3) Set relates_to = N32: the round-2 corrected recommendation asked the row to say "wrap kout, conv[i], and the callback's dest at the call site without copying" [r2-findings.md, N32 design-text-lens correction]; the design answered with "Wrap scratch" only. (4) Suggested row text: "Deduction of Aligned and Dma needs a const_view argument. Construct const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<D>,sseq<1>> explicitly from kout (a writable slice view), from conv[i], from the K callback's dest, and from test arrays, as section 5 requires for V rows. A writable view or a raw pointer does not bind [view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:116-117, 361-362, 373, 382-385]." (5) Cite view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:116-117 (view is final, derives from expr only) and :382, :385 (const_view converting constructors) as the source for the deduction claim.
Narrow the claim to the save side. The load side is already covered: get_k_row takes view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> [design-r3.txt:405], and wrapping the raw scratch pointer (full.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2655 dest; t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:405 got.data()) is what "Wrap scratch without copying" [design-r3.txt:405] asks for; that wrap deduces because it is the same class template. The gap is set_k_row: kout is a writable view [model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2863, :2920; slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:76], the conversion path already holds a writable view<bf16,true,false,seq<D>>{conv[0]} [model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2922-2924], and the layout test holds a non-const std::array at four save sites [t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:401, 424, 428, 453], not one. Drop the sentence "Template argument deduction does not consider conversions" in favor of the precise mechanism: const_view's constructors from view are non-template converting constructors [view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:379-386], which deduction ignores. Do not cite design-r3.txt:401 "const conversion" as covering this: that sentence carries over the owner/view rule of :248, not a call-site rule. Recommended sentence for the :405 row: "Section 5's explicit-construction rule applies to the save side: kout and the conversion scratch view are writable views, so construct const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> explicitly at each set_k_row call (model.hpp store_k_block, t_k_vnni_layout); the load side wraps raw scratch in the writable view as stated."
Three narrowings. (1) Remove "the copy paths" from the recommended caller list. The child's copy_storage_slot never calls set_k_row or get_k_row; it calls k_vnni::gather_row and k_vnni::scatter_row directly, plus memcpy for a whole page [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2107, 2110-2114], and the design routes copies through "typed K token copies and a typed K plane-copy operation" instead [design-r3.txt:406]. (2) Widen the test callers. At the child, t/t_llama_unit.cpp has 29 set_k_row/get_k_row call lines passing .data() pointers (for example :362-366, :392-407, :579-607, :829, :852, :1469, :1490, :1588, :1610, :1951, :2247-2249, :2284-2285, :2331-2333, :2410, :2442, :2724), t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp has 8 (:401, :405, :424, :428, :434, :441, :453, :459), and t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp has 1 (:101 zero.data()). The design's child section names only t_k_vnni_layout for typed row saves [design-r3.txt:414]; the :377 mention of t_llama_unit.cpp and t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp is the parent's V step. (3) Narrow the gap statement. "Wrap scratch without copying" [design-r3.txt:405] already tells the implementer to wrap the raw scratch pointers (full.hpp dest, model.hpp conv[0], the test buffers). What the K row lacks is (a) the writable-view source case at model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2920, where kout is a writable slice view and the caller must write const_view<...>{kout} or wrap kout.data, and (b) the general statement that deduction does not use the view-to-const_view converting constructor or the explicit pointer constructor. Suggested text for the :405 row: "As for V in section 5, construct the read-only source view explicitly at each caller (model.hpp store_k_block both branches, full.hpp k_head_fn, and the set_k_row/get_k_row callers in t_llama_unit, t_k_vnni_layout, and t_amx_dispatch_dtype); deduction of const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,...> does not see a writable view or a raw pointer." Alternatively, generalize :257 to "both row families".
Recommendation
Append to the row: 'As in section 5, construct the read-only source view explicitly at each caller (model.hpp store_k_block, the copy paths, full.hpp's K callback, t_k_vnni_layout); deduction of const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,...> does not see a writable view or a raw pointer.'
Lead's reading
Holds 3 of 3 at note, narrowed to the save side: get_k_row's load view deduces from the wrapped scratch, but set_k_row's const_view parameter cannot deduce from the writable slice view kout (model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2864, :2920), because const_view's constructors from view are converting constructors that deduction ignores. The child's test callers are many more than t_k_vnni_layout: about 29 sites in t_llama_unit.cpp and one in t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp.

child-needs; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Note G47. 8-lane child check: TRON_K_VNNI=OFF is forced, not chosen

Where
7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:414); 8. Build configurations (:461)
Claim

At the child head TRON_K_VNNI=ON fails to configure without TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, and TRON_AMX_DISPATCH fails to compile at 8 lanes. Saying so prevents an implementer from spending a build on the impossible combination and from reporting it as a skipped check.

Where

touches F43, G16, G17
Claim

5. The decision record: Q1 to Q4 and D5

Design section 2 now carries a decision record: four questions for Ben (Q1 to Q4) with a proposed choice, a fallback if rejected and a record status, plus D5, the native-K typing scope that jhan requested. The owner is the issue assignee, the record place is the parent PR description, and implementation may start on the proposed choices labelled Proposed. All three judges found the record adequate. The table gives my reading of each row against Ben's three comments (evidence/github-review.json) and jhan's request.

Question or decision, and the proposed choiceCoversAssessment this round
Q1. May each concrete view contain its address formula? Proposed: yes; one view per layout, separate V and K owners, no layout-policy template.Ben's sketch sections 1 and 2 (layout as a template argument of owner and view) and his own reservation: "I'm not entirely convinced of the necessity of separate k_vnni_layout from vnni_view".Adequate. The proposed choice follows Ben's stated doubt, and the fallback is the sketch itself, so either answer is implementable. The design also confirms jhan's reading of that reservation (design-r3.txt:71); N11 asks it to state what the reading was.
Q2. Should const matrix and row objects prevent element mutation? Proposed: yes at both ranks, following tron's rank-2 view rule.Ben's sketch: "As with std::span, a const view object does not make its elements const. vnni_view<const bf16, Layout> does."Adequate, and the question most likely to be answered no, because Ben stated the opposite rule in writing. The design flags the departure and gives a fallback. Keep the const contracts in one table row so a flip is one edit, and settle the row-conversion clause first (N20, G40).
Q3. May packed views expose logical rows through operator[]? Proposed: yes, returning a logical row expression with no public pointer; keep bounds-checked at().Ben's review asked for the expr precedent ("a uniform interface based upon expr"); his sketch excluded operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row.Adequate. The design resolves the tension between the two comments in favor of expression reads with no pointer route, and names the fallback (remove the row expressions from both families). G12 notes that the mandated bf16_chunk override serves a test-only path.
Q4. Is the smaller parent scope sufficient? Proposed: yes; expression reads and the existing typed bulk operations; slices, iterators and generic writes deferred.Ben's review: "the usual operations that we expect of tensor types and, in particular, the row-wise load/store operations".Adequate. The deferrals are explicit and reasoned. G39 asks to restore the one sentence that mapped append_v_row to the sketch's store_row, so Ben sees the name difference explained; the maintainer judge also asks for a sketch-name to design-name table.
D5. Who owns native-K typing? Requested scope: the issue author; reuse the existing K view type through a new alias and change the native-K kernel parameter.jhan's request ("introduces the new abstraction on VNNI-V and native K") and the issue title; not part of Ben's sketch.Right attribution, one wording slip: the cell says "reuse the existing view alias", but const_native_k_view is new in section 5; the view type is what exists (G04). The record is copied into the PR description, so fix the cell before implementation.
What the record still lacksjhan's step-4 premise (the child's packed-K change becomes a local change within the new type) and the identity of "the issue author", "the assignee" and "the user".Two notes: the premise is answered nowhere, and it holds only in part (G37, N56 residual); the three role labels for one person are never tied together (G35). Neither blocks implementation.

Nothing in the record misquotes Ben. The maintainer judge checked the comment ids and times against the saved JSON and found one phrase stronger than his words: "the reviewer's uniform-expression requirement" (design-r3.txt:412) where he wrote "I think the way I would approach this".

6. What the revision gets right

  • design-r3.txt:148 labels 2880c3aa9b as saved review evidence and orders a fresh fetch before branching. This matches the live state: main moved to 1400fa4481 with no change to any parent step file (facts-r3.md:38-43).
  • design-r3.txt:232-234 gives const_native_k_view explicit strides. That alias is the exact type of the const kv_block::k_view(): row_major<seq<R,C>> is sseq<C,1> (seq.hpp@2880c3aa9b:388-394), and const_view(view const&) at view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:299 converts the writable page::k result.
  • design-r3.txt:267 names pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head) as the whole-plane replacement for k0.data. That two-argument overload exists (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1559-1569) and the caller it replaces is self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1652-1655.
  • design-r3.txt:257 says the aligned flag preserves the existing aligned load/store contract. That is right: set_v loads the source with _mm256_load_epi32 and _mm512_load_ps (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2300, 2308, 2339, 2380) and get_v stores with _mm512_store_ps and _mm256_store_si256 (2421, 2455). Every existing test buffer except heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441 is alignas(64) or a tensor.
  • design-r3.txt:344-347 builds the arena on placement array-new of a trivially default-constructible block. That holds: bf16s is __m256i (simd/types.hpp@2880c3aa9b:26, auto.hpp:54), and kv_block holds only k, v and member functions (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2191-2241).
  • design-r3.txt:362-363 lists ten comment sites for the V layout. A grep for 'interleav' over h, src and t at 2880c3aa9b finds no V-layout comment outside those ten sites and the set_v and append_v bodies that move with their code.
  • design-r3.txt:452 says the 16-lane AMX-off row matches the required Nix feature combination. Verified: nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix@2880c3aa9b:79-88 passes -DBUILD_NATIVE=OFF and -DAVX512=ON and no TRON_AMX_DISPATCH.
  • design-r3.txt:454 and :457 keep t_amx_dispatch_dtype as the 8-lane target and mark the rest 'Insufficient data'. The target is sound: exercise_page is a function template (t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:62-63) and both TEST_CASEs sit under #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (:192-214), so its unguarded set_v at :98 is never instantiated at 8 lanes.
  • design-r3.txt:477 calls make iwyu-check optional. That holds beyond the SKILL.md rule: the lint recipe (GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:693-731), the Nix lint check body and gcp-nix.yml contain no IWYU step.
  • design-r3.txt:424 names the direct three-thread calls in t/t_llama_unit.cpp. The child section at t/t_llama_unit.cpp@30c4ac82cb:2773-2782 calls save_k_helper for workers 1 and 2 and save_k(..., N_WORKERS_3) for worker 0, which the 'old positional calls' sentence catches.
  • design-r3.txt:486 names gen/t_llama_unit. CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:66 sets CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY to the binary directory and the native preset's binaryDir is gen (CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:34).
  • design-r3.txt:148 says 98bb8cb2 is 79 commits after f46e48ba. git rev-list --count f46e48bab498..98bb8cb22f94 returns 79.
  • design-r3.txt:583-584 S19 and S20 anchors point at the right code: test_runtime.py:30-51 (test_identity, passed_sentinel), slice_bench_manifest.py:14 (STANDARD_BENCH_WIDTHS = (1, 2, 4)), bin/slice:1996-2000 (capacity check), :1731-1736 (put_platform_bench), :3676-3684 (whole-box bench), .github/AGENTS.md:19-20.
  • design-r3.txt:269 says the v_head_fn callback wraps its aligned scratch. src/tron/gof.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203 declares alignas(64) std::array<bf16, ...> v_scratch and :213 passes per-head offsets of HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE elements, so the destination view's alignment precondition holds.
  • Section 9 lists every one of the 64 round-2 ids and the 58 retained round-1 ids exactly once, and its rejected-candidate sentence names the right 10 plus 6 ids (G52, a positive check by the section-9 auditor).
  • The NaN bit table is right under all three conversion paths; the lead recomputed 0x7F800001 to 0x7F80, 0x7F81 and 0x7F80.

8. Verdict

Three judges read the design and the review summary independently. Their verdicts agree on accept with changes and split on timing: the implementing agent would start today and stop once, at step 2, on G01; the maintainer and the risk reviewer would not start until G01 and G22 are written. The lead's verdict follows the majority: accept with changes, not yet implementable as written; two sentences away from implementable.

implementable now		I could start building the parent from this page today. The forward declarations, the owner, the view, the row expression, and the three typed operations (append_v_row, load_row, copy_token) have declared signatures and stated semantics [design-r3.txt:232-260]. not yet implementable		The design answers all three of my comments. Comment 1 (the worker index, r4065141577) is answered by merging save_k and save_k_helper into one save_k(..., worker_ix = 0, n_workers = 1) and deleting the helper entry points [design-r3.txt:420-424]; the child's two generated call sites exist where the design says (save_k_helper at ingest/src/TronCpp.hs@30c4ac82cb:2338, save_k at :2406). Comment 2 (review 5270587330) asked for an expr-based packed tensor with row-wise load and store operations; the design gives v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view and v_vnni_row that inherit expr, plus append_v_row, load_row and copy_token [design-r3.txt:238-240, 255], and it types both kernel arguments [design-r3.txt:277]. Comment 3 (my sketch, 5765866077) is followed on every point I would insist on: an aligned owner with lvalue-only as_view [design-r3.txt:238-239], private pointer construction with mutable-to-const conversion only [design-r3.txt:248], bounds-checked at() [design-r3.txt:245], the arena constructs the real storage type and never casts the old SIMD array [design-r3.txt:344-352], no clearing is added [design-r3.txt:118, 346], k_storage is selected by std::conditional_t [design-r3.txt:399], and the page accessors and the AVX-512 fallback take typed views [design-r3.txt:404, 407]. Where the design departs from the sketch it says so and asks: the address formula lives in the view (Q1, which matches my own stated doubt), const applies at both ranks (Q2), operator[] returns a non-contiguous logical row (Q3), and the first scope is smaller (Q4) [design-r3.txt:95-98]. Nothing is attributed to me that I did not write: the ids and times in the table match the saved JSON (18:24:52; 18:53:47; 18:58:31 edited 18:59:58) [design-r3.txt:67-69; evidence/github-review.json], "drafted his GitHub sketch with his own Claude session" paraphrases my "Claude and I came up with the following straw-man" [design-r3.txt:46], and every proposal is labelled "Proposed; reviewer answer not recorded" [design-r3.txt:95-99]. One phrase is stronger than what I wrote: "the reviewer's uniform-expression requirement" [design-r3.txt:412]; my review said "I think the way I would approach this". The one gap I would not let implementation start with is my own boundary rule applied to scaled_v_expr. The design says the internal accessor is for typed operations and V-specific kernels only and that cache callers do not use it [design-r3.txt:249]. It also says scaled_v_expr obtains the physical address only inside the typed implementation [design-r3.txt:329]. But scaled_v_expr is cache code in kv_cache.hpp that dereferences a raw bf16s* today [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1990-1993, 2087-2088], and the design does not say which rule wins. My sketch allowed "SIMD and AMX implementations" to use the helper, so the design should say that scaled_v_expr is such an implementation, or it should move the body and list that move. The same silence covers the t_amx_numerics PV fixture, which passes a std::vector<bf16> pointer to weights_times_v_128x4 today [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203-227] and has no permitted typed route under the design's rules (no public pointer construction, no test use of the helper). Smaller slips: D5 calls const_native_k_view "the existing view alias" [design-r3.txt:99], but the alias is new in kv_cache_fwd.hpp [design-r3.txt:232]; the type it names is the existing one (const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<R,C>> with the row_major default stride sseq<C,1>, h/tron/tensor/views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:31-36, kv_cache.hpp:2215-2219). The accessor names are not parallel (page::v_view versus page::k_packed [design-r3.txt:271, 404]) where my sketch used v_packed and k_packed. The headers diverge from my single h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp into v_vnni.hpp and k_vnni.hpp [design-r3.txt:391]; round 2 judged that no change required (N59), and I accept it as the design's choice. The verdict is accept with changes: the interface and the decision record are what I asked for, and two short text edits settle the only questions an implementer could get wrong.Settle where the packed PV weighted sum obtains its address. In the page::scaled_v row [design-r3.txt:273] and step 2 [design-r3.txt:373] write: 'detail::scaled_v_expr stays in kv_cache.hpp. Its 16-lane constructor takes v_vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, head_size>, obtains the pair-tile base once through detail::v_vnni_access, and keeps today's _mm512_load_si512 and _mm512_dpbf16_ps loops (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2066-2135). It is a V-specific SIMD implementation, which the sketch allows to use the helper.' not yet implementable		The design now states the numerics, lifetime and concurrency rules an implementer needs, and I checked the load-bearing ones against source. The NaN table is right: fp32 0x7F800001 truncates to bf16 0x7F80 (+infinity) in the packed append, and the generic conversion gives 0x7F81 (a NaN) only when TRON_IGNORE_NAN is absent [design-r3.txt:334; h/common/numerics/bf16.hpp@f46e48ba:92-109]. The existing padding test poisons the odd row through page::set_v with a float quiet NaN (0x7FC00000, which truncates to 0x7FC0 and stays a NaN), so the design's poison route survives the removal of v_data [design-r3.txt:436; t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:2776-2792]. Today's packed set_v already uses aligned source loads (_mm256_load_epi32 for bf16 and fp16 rows, _mm512_load_ps for float rows), so the design's 64-byte aligned-source rule preserves an existing precondition rather than adding a new crash risk [design-r3.txt:257; kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2300-2312, :2380]. The constructor sets storage_slot_count_ in its initializer list before it calls allocate_kv_group, so slot enumeration inside the construction helper sees the right slot count [design-r3.txt:348; kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1091-1103]. A placement array-new probe on a trivially destructible 64-byte-aligned 32,768-byte block returned the same address as the arena at -O0 and -O2 with g++ 11.4 (clang 19, the pinned compiler, is not available on this host), so the construction form at design-r3.txt:344 is low risk. Pair-write serialization and one-lane-per-line K stores are stated [design-r3.txt:318-319, :410]. Two gaps stop a compiling first draft. detail::scaled_v_expr lives in kv_cache.hpp, reads the pair-tile base through reinterpret_cast of a raw bf16s pointer, and receives kv_block.v[0] from page::scaled_v [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2016-2017, :2085-2086, :2260-2261]. The design forbids cache code from using detail::v_vnni_access, requires a packed view in that constructor, and names no route from the view to the tile base [design-r3.txt:249, :273, :329]. The typed parameter has no pointer route, and the design names no typed route for this test [design-r3.txt:277, :377]. One CI risk needs a stronger done criterion. The kernel call sites in self_attention.hpp are inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1571, :1654, :1680]. The required Nix lane never sets that option [nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix@2880c3aa9b:79-102]. The fakes at t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@2880c3aa9b:47-190 compile in every build, so required CI catches a declaration mismatch but not a broken typed caller. The design asks for the AMX-on build [design-r3.txt:453], but the definition of done only says that build is reported separately [design-r3.txt:498]. What changed since round 2. Round 2 named four major gaps; all four are closed or reduced to wording (N01 resolved, N02 resolved, N03 resolved with G16 and G17 as the header-home residue, N04 resolved). The one major of this round is new: it appeared when the round-3 edit deleted the sentence that kept scaled_v_expr in kv_cache.hpp while tightening the access rule. Every other new finding is a wording, citation or process item, and the checkers found no regression among the 87 known items.

What the design does not have to do. It compiled nothing and ran nothing; it posted nothing and edited no issue; sw-dev-01 is codex's user rule. None of those is a finding. Ben's answers to Q1 to Q4 are not needed to start: the design says to implement the proposed choices and label them Proposed in the PR description, and all three judges accept that.

10. Method, evidence, and limits

This round used the same shape as round 2, with more finders. The lead first re-verified the facts the design relies on (comment-site line ranges, CMake and Nix flags, workflow steps, child names) against the pinned commits and refreshed the live state of main, issue #4525 and PR 4424; that record is evidence/claude-review-r3/lead-verified-facts-r3.md.

Numbers of this round: 234 agents, 28.2 million tokens, 2 hours 15 minutes; 17 disposition batches (51 agents), 10 finders, 1 merge agent, 52 merged candidates with 156 verifier votes, 1 critic with 4 candidates and 12 votes, 3 judges. The workflow result is evidence/claude-review-r3/workflow-result.json; the inner result (the script's return value) is workflow-result-inner.json.

  • Dispositions: 17 batches of 3 to 7 items; per batch one checker and two re-graders (source-truth, fairness); majority of three; batch files in evidence/claude-review-r3/batches/.
  • New findings: 10 finders (C++ interface, behavior and arena construction, process and CI, handoff actionability, fidelity to Ben and jhan, simplicity and scope, formulas and page quality, the child's needs, regression hunt on the round-2 to round-3 diff, section-9 honesty audit), plus new defects the checkers found in the fixes; one merge agent deduplicated; three verifiers (source, design-text fairness, intent) per candidate; a finding survived when at least two could not refute it; the lead read every vote before grading.
  • Completeness critic after verification; its candidates went through the same three verifiers.
  • Three judges (implementer, maintainer, risk) read the design and the review summary independently.
  • Limits: no code was compiled or run; no chromium on this host, so the page was checked structurally (ASCII, anchors, links) and not rendered; agents can make mistakes, so every finding carries its evidence and votes; the lead's own reading is not a substitute for Ben's.
  • Evidence: evidence/claude-review-r3/ holds the workflow result, findings.json, build_findings.py, gen_review.py, overrides.json, the design text and word diff, the facts file and the batch files.
